The linuxptp-daemon pods (`linuxptp-daemon-4stqt`, `linuxptp-daemon-2gr6s`) either restarted mid-test — cutting the log stream — or ptp4l never completed Best-Master-Clock-Algorithm selection because the upstream GNSS/GM clock was not providing a valid signal during this run. The test helper (`GetForeignClockID`, called from `ptp.go`) streams container logs via the Kubernetes API with a ~200 s deadline and fails when the expected regex never appears. This failure is cross-suite (identical errors in dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc suites against different daemon pods), ruling out a single-pod crash as the cause and pointing to a cluster-wide sync problem. -
Full analysis
--- ## Cross-Suite Summary: periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-4.20-e2e-telco5g-ptp **Job:** `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-4.20-e2e-telco5g-ptp` **Version:** 4.20 | **Total failures:** 26 | **Suites:** bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc, tgm --- **TL;DR:** GNSS-locked GM node `cnfdf32` never left FREERUN (ClockClass 248), cascading timeouts across all 6 suites. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **25/26 failures — GM stuck in FREERUN (ClockClass 248), cascading to all downstream suites** Suites: tgm (3), bc (4), dualfollower (4), dualnicbc (4), dualnicbcha (4), tbc (6 of 7) The T-GM grandmaster node (`cnfdf32`, interface `ens7fx`) never achieved or re-acquired GNSS lock (ClockClass 7 / PRTC_LOCKED) during this run. All downstream BC and OC nodes depend on this GM. Because `ptp4l` / `ts2phc` on `cnfdf32` stayed at ClockClass 248 (FREERUN), no follower node ever emitted the expected `selected best master clock` or foreign-clock-ID log lines. Every test that polls `linuxptp-daemon-container` via a Kubernetes log-stream regex watcher timed out, producing either `timedout waiting for matches` (~205–237 s) or `error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded` (Kubernetes API killed the stream). The three tgm tests directly exercised GNSS manipulation (`ubxtool -z CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS`, ts2phc kill/restart) and confirmed the GM never entered holdover; the metric `ens7fx/master:248` was reported instead of the expected `7.000000`. - **1/26 failure — TBC ptpconfig SCHED_FIFO / test-code mismatch** Suite: tbc (1 of 7) The TBC ptpconfig includes `SCHED_FIFO` scheduling for ptp4l/phc2sys processes, but the `main`-branch test (running against `release-4.20` images per `PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.20`) asserts that no real-time FIFO processes should be present. This is a suite-specific test/config skew independent of the GM state. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Suite | GM FREERUN cascade | Suite-specific | |---|---|---| | tgm | 3 (direct GNSS/holdover failures) | — | | bc | 4 | — | | dualfollower | 4 | — | | dualnicbc | 4 | — | | dualnicbcha | 4 | — | | tbc | 6 | 1 (SCHED_FIFO mismatch) | All suites share the same underlying GM FREERUN failure. The tgm suite is the **origin** (GNSS-layer failure on `cnfdf32`); bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, and tbc are **downstream casualties**. Only tbc has one additional suite-specific failure unrelated to the GM state. The cross-suite daemon pod list (`linuxptp-daemon-4stqt`, `2gr6s`, `6xl5p`, `vfnqw`, `5lj7k`, `pb4kk`, `jmj9c`, `57h79`, `zqkqd`) spans every affected node, confirming this is cluster-wide, not a single pod crash. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Retry the job immediately.** The `investigation-summary.txt` pre-classification is `Fix type: infra`. If the retry passes cleanly, this is a transient GNSS hardware event on the `cnfdf32` telco5g lab node. No code change is needed. 2. **Audit the GNSS receiver on `cnfdf32` around the run window (2026-07-28 ~02:07–05:30 UTC).** The tgm suite started at 02:07:08 and `checkStabilityOfWPCGMUsingMetrics` passed initially — the GM was locked at job start — but all three GNSS manipulation tests confirm it never re-acquired lock after the signal was disturbed. Check whether the WPC card's GNSS antenna feed, the `ts2phc` holdover duration setting, or the `ubxtool` threshold command left the receiver in a permanently degraded state for the remainder of the job. 3. **Fix the TBC SCHED_FIFO test/config skew.** Either update the `release-4.20` TBC ptpconfig to remove `SCHED_FIFO`, or update the `main`-branch test assertion to tolerate FIFO processes when the ptpconfig declares them. This 1/26 failure will persist independently of GM health. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `infra_timeout` **Overall Severity:** HIGH — all 6 test suites failed in a single nightly run; the E2E signal for OCP 4.20 telco5g PTP is completely dark for this build, masking any real regressions that may exist. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 bc failures stem from PTP synchronization never establishing on the lab nodes — the linuxptp-daemon log stream either closed early or never produced a master-clock-selected log line, and the parallel event test confirmed the OS clock never reached LOCKED state. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream unreachable / no sync log output** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-4stqt container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` ``` could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-4stqt container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` From the CI build log, `ClockClassChangeIndicator = "selected best master clock"` (`linuxptp-daemon/pkg/daemon/daemon.go:65`) is the string the test regex hunts for in the streaming container log. Two of the three tests received `context deadline exceeded` (the k8s log stream was forcibly closed, consistent with the pod crashing or restarting). The third received `timedout waiting for matches` (stream was open but `ptp4l` never emitted `selected best master clock`, meaning PTP BMCA never completed). - **Root Cause:** The linuxptp-daemon pods (`linuxptp-daemon-4stqt`, `linuxptp-daemon-2gr6s`) either restarted mid-test — cutting the log stream — or ptp4l never completed Best-Master-Clock-Algorithm selection because the upstream GNSS/GM clock was not providing a valid signal during this run. The test helper (`GetForeignClockID`, called from `ptp.go`) streams container logs via the Kubernetes API with a ~200 s deadline and fails when the expected regex never appears. This failure is cross-suite (identical errors in dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc suites against different daemon pods), ruling out a single-pod crash as the cause and pointing to a cluster-wide sync problem. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown. The only recent main-branch commits are linuxptp-daemon `14eb788` (upstream-sync-2026-07-22, adds CI/CD scaffolding only) and cloud-event-proxy `0a69e46` (ntpThresholds, main only). Per the branch-awareness rule, neither is in `release-4.20` images; neither can be the root cause. No cherry-pick found. - **Is it a flake?** yes - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the job. If the pattern repeats across multiple nightly runs, investigate whether the GNSS signal source for the telco5g lab (cnfdf3x nodes) was degraded around 2026-07-28T02–05Z, or whether the linuxptp-daemon DaemonSet pods on those nodes had OOM/restart events during the test window. - **Group 2: PTP OS clock never reached LOCKED state** (affects: "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync") - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` ``` PtpEventsIsConsumerReady= false ``` (From build log, parallel suite config at 05:40:45Z) - **Root Cause:** The event-based parallel test polls for the OS clock to enter `PTP_LOCKED` state within its deadline. `PtpEventsIsConsumerReady= false` at test start means the cloud-event-proxy sidecar had not yet signaled readiness; combined with PTP sync not establishing (Group 1), the clock never transitioned to LOCKED. This is the direct observable symptom of the same environment-level sync failure. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown (same reasoning as Group 1 — no release-4.20 commit change identified). - **Is it a flake?** yes - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1. Additionally confirm the cloud-event-proxy sidecar is starting cleanly by checking DaemonSet restart counts in a subsequent run's `must-gather` output. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_timeout **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## dualfollower --- ### Suite: dualfollower (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All four dualfollower tests failed because the linuxptp-daemon pod (`linuxptp-daemon-6xl5p`) never produced the expected sync-state log lines the test framework polls, a cross-suite systemic symptom indicating PTP synchronization was never established in this test run. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream returns no matching profile line** (affects: "Slave can sync to master [Serial]", "Dual follower can sync when one follower port goes down [Serial]", "Can provide a profile with higher priority [Serial]") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-6xl5p container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-6xl5p container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` Cross-suite confirmation (identical error against different pods): `linuxptp-daemon-4stqt` (bc), `linuxptp-daemon-vfnqw` (dualnicbc), `linuxptp-daemon-pb4kk` (dualnicbcha), `linuxptp-daemon-57h79` (tbc). - **Root Cause:** The test framework streams `linuxptp-daemon-container` logs and scans for a regex matching the selected foreign clock / profile line (indicating PTP sync is established). The daemon on the follower node never emits that line within the 202s timeout, meaning ptp4l on the OC/follower never selects a foreign master. This is a systemic failure: every suite's serial ClockSync tests fail against every daemon pod with the same error, ruling out a dualfollower-specific code path and pointing to PTP synchronization not being achieved across the entire lab cluster in this run. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown. Images are built from `openshift/ptp-operator:release-4.20`; the recent linuxptp-daemon upstream sync commit (`14eb788`, merged 2026-07-22, main-only) is not in the production images since `USE_UPSTREAM=false` per the build log (`+ PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.20`). No cherry-pick evidence available. - **Is it a flake?** Yes — the cross-suite pattern (bc, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc, dualfollower all hitting the same error simultaneously against independent pods) and the `investigation-summary.txt` "Fix type: infra" classification both indicate a transient PTP environment/hardware issue rather than a code regression. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the job; if this recurs in consecutive runs, inspect the physical/virtual PTP grandmaster (`test-grandmaster`) and the network path between it and the follower nodes — the `DiscoveredMasterInterfaces=[]` in the test config output (`testconfig.go:940`) suggests the grandmaster-side interface was not discovered, which would prevent any follower from locking. - **Group 2: OS Clock never LOCKED** (affects: "PTP Slave Clock Sync [parallel]") - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` The same message appears in the parallel event test for every other suite (bc-parallel, dualnicbc-parallel, dualnicbcha-parallel, tbc-parallel). - **Root Cause:** This is a direct consequence of the same root cause as Group 1. The event-based parallel test waits for the OS clock to reach LOCKED state (i.e., phc2sys successfully steering the system clock from ptp4l). Since ptp4l never selected a foreign master (Group 1), phc2sys has no source and the OS clock never locks. The `PtpEventsIsConsumerReady= false` line in the test config also confirms the event sidecar (cloud-event-proxy) had no LOCKED event to publish. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown (same systemic cause as Group 1). - **Is it a flake?** Yes — same environment-level cause as Group 1. - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1; resolving the PTP grandmaster connectivity will also resolve this test. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_other **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM (all 4 tests failed, but cross-suite evidence strongly indicates a transient lab environment issue, not a regression) ## dualnicbc --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 dualnicbc tests failed because the `linuxptp-daemon-container` never produced the expected synchronization log output (foreign clock ID / LOCKED state), which is the same infrastructure-level PTP sync failure observed across every test suite in this job run. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream produces no sync evidence** (affects: "Slave can sync to master [Serial]", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master [Serial]", "Can provide a profile with higher priority [Serial]") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-vfnqw container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-5lj7k container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-vfnqw container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` The test helper (sourced from `main` branch test scripts) opens a Kubernetes log stream against `linuxptp-daemon-container` and scans it for a regex that confirms a foreign master clock ID has been received. Two distinct sub-errors appear: `context deadline exceeded` (the Kubernetes log-stream API call itself timed out) and `timedout waiting for matches` (stream was open but the daemon never emitted the expected log line within the test timeout of ~205–237 s). - **Root Cause:** The `linuxptp-daemon` (built from `release-4.20` images, `PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.20`) never achieved PTP synchronization with its upstream master during this run, so the log lines that encode the foreign clock ID (`GetForeignClockID` regex) were never written. The identical error pattern appears across every other suite in this same job (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbcha, tbc), all pointing at the same underlying hardware/network-level PTP failure rather than a code regression in any single suite. No image build errors were found in the build log; `USE_UPSTREAM=false` confirms production `release-4.20` images were used. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code-level change is implicated; this is a cross-suite infrastructure failure. - **Is it a flake?** yes — the cross-suite pattern (every suite failing the same way in the same run, while infrastructure/nodes were Ready) is characteristic of a transient hardware or PTP network issue on the telco5g baremetal lab. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the periodic job; if the pattern persists across multiple nightly runs, investigate PTP grandmaster hardware health and network connectivity on the `cnfdf3*` lab nodes. - **Group 2: OS clock never reached LOCKED state** (affects: "PTP Slave Clock Sync [dualnicbc-parallel]") - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` The same pod (`linuxptp-daemon-vfnqw`/`linuxptp-daemon-5lj7k`) that failed to produce foreign-clock-ID log output in Group 1 also failed to ever report an OS clock LOCKED event to the event-based parallel test. The parallel suite uses a different code path (event subscription) but depends on the same physical PTP sync condition. - **Root Cause:** Direct consequence of Group 1 — because the daemon never achieved PTP synchronization, no LOCKED clock-class event was ever published by the cloud-event-proxy sidecar, so the event-based test consumed its full timeout waiting for a lock that never came. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — same infrastructure root cause as Group 1. - **Is it a flake?** yes — same transient infrastructure failure as Group 1. - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1; the event-based test will self-heal once the underlying PTP sync issue is resolved. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_other **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## dualnicbcha --- ### Suite: dualnicbcha (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 failures are caused by a cluster-wide PTP hardware/GNSS outage that prevented the linuxptp-daemon from producing expected sync log output, making every test that reads from the daemon log stream time out with `context deadline exceeded`. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream context deadline exceeded** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "phc2sys switches to secondary ptp4l when primary interface fails", "PTP Slave Clock Sync [parallel]") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-pb4kk container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-jmj9c container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` From `junit-failures.txt`, the identical error structure (`error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded`) hits every suite on this run: `bc`, `dualfollower`, `dualnicbc`, `dualnicbcha`, `tbc` — across distinct daemon pods (pb4kk, jmj9c, 4stqt, 2gr6s, vfnqw, 5lj7k, 6xl5p, 57h79, zqkqd). `testSyncState()` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` subscribes to `OsClockSyncStateChange` events via the `cloud-event-proxy` sidecar, calls `event.PushInitialEvent()` (which succeeded — otherwise line 378 would have fired), then waits up to 5 minutes for any event carrying `state == "LOCKED"`. The timer (`tcEndChan`) expired before `wasLocked` was set to `true`, meaning the initial event published a non-LOCKED state (FREERUN or HOLDOVER) and the clock did not transition to LOCKED within 5 minutes. The fact that all 6 parallel suites fail the same event-based clock sync test simultaneously while all bc serial tests pass is the defining signal: this is a cross-mode systemic PTP environment instability, not a bc-specific code regression. A relevant contextual factor is commit `4aa5310` (`ci: fix timeout onPushInitialEvent`, Aug 2025) which notes that OCPBUGS-60570 changed when `OsClockSyncStateChange` events can be pulled from the socket — this fix is on `main` (test code), but the `
Full analysis
**TL;DR:** A `main`-branch test key-naming regression breaks all 6 parallel event-sync tests; 4 tgm-serial failures stem from unported daemon features. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **~6/16 failures — `createStoredEvent()` key-change regression breaks `testSyncState()` in all parallel suites** (`[bc-parallel]`, `[dualfollower-parallel]`, `[dualnicbc-parallel]`, `[dualnicbcha-parallel]`, `[oc-parallel]`, `[tbc-parallel]` — all: `Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) Commit `d20ffcb` (2026-07-03) / PR #711 commit `5e9ecf0` (2026-07-15) changed `createStoredEvent()` in `test/pkg/event/event.go` to store values under `v.Resource` (e.g. `/cluster/node/…/CLOCK_REALTIME`) instead of `v.DataType` (`"notification"`). `testSyncState()` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` still reads `values["notification"].(string)` — that key is now always absent. `state` is permanently `""`, `wasLocked` never flips, and every parallel event suite exhausts its configured window (~6 min) and fails deterministically at `ptp.go:393`. - **1/16 failures — ts2phc `process_restart_count` reset on PtpConfig delete/re-apply** (`[tgm-serial] Verify ts2phc metrics are cleaned up after GM PtpConfig delete and re-apply`) Commit `be1eb0d3` (2026-06-28) added a `main`-only CI test asserting the restart counter is monotonically non-decreasing across a delete/re-apply cycle. The linuxptp-daemon unconditionally deletes the `CounterVec` label set in `DeleteProcessStatusMetrics` (`cleanup.go:58`), resetting the counter to 1. The corresponding daemon fix does not exist in `release-4.22` or `main`, so the assertion fails every run. - **3/16 failures — T-GM ClockClass 7 holdover window too brief for detection** (`[tgm-serial]` "Testing WPC T-GM holdover through connection loss", "Continuously terminating ts2phc triggers FREERUN event and then recovers to LOCKED", "Testing T-GM Events on GNSS reboot and recovery") After GNSS disable, the hardware transitions through ClockClass 7 (holdover) transiently before reaching 248 (FREERUN). The polling interval and event-window size in the `main`-branch test code are too coarse to catch this transition against `release-4.22` images, which may move through holdover more quickly than assumed. By the time the test polls, ptp4l is already at class 248. - **~6/16 failures — origin unclear from provided analyses** (may be additional serial failures or unanalyzed suite variants) --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Pattern | Suites affected | Suite-specific? | |---|---|---| | `values["notification"]` key mismatch | bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc, tbc (all parallel) | No — cross-suite, same root cause | | ts2phc restart-count assertion | tgm only | Yes | | T-GM ClockClass 7 holdover window | tgm only | Yes | All 6 parallel suites share a single root cause (the key-change regression in `test/pkg/event/event.go`). Serial suites in bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, oc passed their clock-sync tests — confirming PTP hardware sync was functional and only the event-reporting/test-parsing path was broken. The tgm failures are entirely independent and suite-specific. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Fix `testSyncState()` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` (highest impact — unblocks ~6 failures):** Update the `values["notification"]` and `values["metric"]` lookups to use the ResourceAddress key emitted by `cloud-event-proxy` (e.g. look up by iterating the map or by a named constant matching the resource path format), or add a `GetValueByDataType(dataType)` helper in `test/pkg/event/event.go` so all consumers are decoupled from the raw key format. This single change restores all 6 parallel event-based suites. 2. **Gate the ts2phc restart-count test behind a daemon capability check or skip it on `release-4.22` (fixes 1 failure):** The test in `ptp.go:2628` was merged to `main` before the linuxptp-daemon fix exists. Either implement and cherry-pick the daemon-side counter-preservation fix to `release-4.22`, or add a version/feature guard so the assertion is skipped when running against images that do not support it. 3. **Extend T-GM ClockClass 7 detection polling in tgm serial tests (fixes 3 failures):** Increase the poll interval tolerance or use event-based detection (subscribing to `ClockClassChange` events) rather than log-polling to observe the holdover transition. The current test assumes a minimum holdover window that `release-4.22` images do not guarantee. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` **Overall Severity:** HIGH — 10+ of 16 failures are deterministic regressions introduced by `main`-branch test code changes that were not paired with matching updates to all call sites or with the required daemon fixes; every run will reproduce these failures until the test code is corrected. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single bc-parallel failure is the "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" test timing out because the BC slave OS clock was never observed in LOCKED state within the 5-minute test window. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: BC slave OS clock never reached LOCKED state within test window** (affects: `[bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** - From `investigation-summary.txt`: `Source: ptp.go:393, run.go:84` - From `junit-failures.txt`: `Test: [It] [bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync — Message: result: error` - From `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:393`: `Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out")` - From `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:386–392` (tcEndChan branch): ```go case <-tcEndChan: if !wasLocked { Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out") } ``` - From `test/conformance/config/ptptestconfig.yaml`: `slave_clock_sync.spec.duration: 5` (5-minute window), `slave_clock_sync.spec.failure_threshold: 10` - From `ci-operator-build-log.txt`: `+ export PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.22` and `+ export TEST_BRANCH=main` — test scripts from `main`, production images from `release-4.22` - From `junit-failures.txt`: The **identical test fails in all 6 parallel suites simultaneously** — `[bc-parallel]`, `[dualfollower-parallel]`, `[dualnicbc-parallel]`, `[dualnicbcha-parallel]`, `[oc-parallel]`, `[tbc-parallel]` — all with `result: error`. Serial bc suite had `failures="0"` (78 tests passed). - **Root Cause:** `testSyncState()` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` subscribes to `OsClockSyncStateChange` events via the `cloud-event-proxy` sidecar, calls `event.PushInitialEvent()` (which succeeded — otherwise line 378 would have fired), then waits up to 5 minutes for any event carrying `state == "LOCKED"`. The timer (`tcEndChan`) expired before `wasLocked` was set to `true`, meaning the initial event published a non-LOCKED state (FREERUN or HOLDOVER) and the clock did not transition to LOCKED within 5 minutes. The fact that all 6 parallel suites fail the same event-based clock sync test simultaneously while all bc serial tests pass is the defining signal: this is a cross-mode systemic PTP environment instability, not a bc-specific code regression. A relevant contextual factor is commit `4aa5310` (`ci: fix timeout onPushInitialEvent`, Aug 2025) which notes that OCPBUGS-60570 changed when `OsClockSyncStateChange` events can be pulled from the socket — this fix is on `main` (test code), but the `release-4.22` production images may have that bug fix applied, meaning the event socket behavior is correct; the issue is the underlying clock sync state at test time. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no `check_backport.py` available and repos are shallow main-branch clones; the cross-suite pattern points to a transient PTP environment condition rather than a specific code commit. Related context: OCPBUGS-60570 (event socket behavior, fixed in `4aa5310` on main). - **Is it a flake?** yes — The simultaneous failure of all 6 parallel `PTP Slave Clock Sync` tests while all serial bc tests pass is the hallmark of a transient PTP grandmaster or GPS-signal instability causing all downstream clocks to remain in FREERUN during the parallel test window. The `[tgm-serial]` test suite also shows failures for `WPC GM GNSS signal loss tests` and `ts2phc termination tests` (which intentionally disrupt the grandmaster), and the 5-minute test window is narrow enough that a temporary GPS/upstream disruption could prevent all parallel suites from observing a LOCKED transition. - **Suggested Fix:** Re-run the periodic job to confirm this is a transient flake. If it recurs consistently, check the `cloud-event-proxy` sidecar logs on `release-4.22` images for FREERUN/HOLDOVER reasons, and verify whether the GNSS/TGM disruption tests in `[tgm-serial]` are sharing timing infrastructure with the BC test nodes. **Suite Issue Class:** test_flake **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## dualfollower --- ### Suite: dualfollower (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" failure is a deterministic test regression introduced by commit `d20ffcb` on `main` (July 3, 2026), which broke how PTP sync-state events are looked up in the test's key-value store, causing all parallel-mode suites to never detect a LOCKED clock state. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: `createStoredEvent` key-change regression breaks `values["notification"]` lookup** (affects: `[dualfollower-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** - JUnit result: `"[It] [dualfollower-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync": {"time": 363.898387443, "result": "error"}` — test ran its full ~6-minute configured duration before dying. - From commit `d20ffcb` diff (`test/pkg/event/event.go`, lines 711–716 and 742–751 as shipped): ```go // Use ResourceAddress as key to avoid collisions… key := v.Resource // e.g. "/cluster/node1/ptp/CLOCK_REALTIME" if key == "" { key = string(v.DataType) } values[key] = v.Value ``` - `cloud-event-proxy/plugins/ptp_operator/metrics/manager.go:319–324` always sets `Resource: eventSource` (non-empty path like `/cluster/node1/ptp/CLOCK_REALTIME`) and `DataType: NOTIFICATION` for the sync-state value. - `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` `testSyncState()` reads: `state, _ := values["notification"].(string)` — this key is now **never populated** because the storage key is the resource path, not `"notification"`. `wasLocked` stays false for the full test duration. - CI build log confirms `TEST_BRANCH=main` — test scripts are from `main`, which includes `d20ffcb`. - **Root Cause:** Commit `d20ffcb` (July 3, 2026) refactored `createStoredEvent` in `test/pkg/event/event.go` to use `v.Resource` as the map key (to handle BC-mode multi-instance collisions), but the cloud-event-proxy always emits a non-empty `Resource` field (a full cluster resource path). The existing `testSyncState` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` still reads `values["notification"]` (the old `DataType`-based key), which is now empty for every event. Consequently, `state` is always `""`, `wasLocked` never flips to `true`, and the test exhausts its 6-minute window before failing at `ptp.go:393`. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** `d20ffcb` (`CNF-19588: CI test for clockClass recovery after cloud-event-proxy crash`) — merged `main` 2026-07-03. Not cherry-picked to `release-4.22`; this is purely a test-code regression on `main`. - **Is it a flake?** No — deterministic. The storage key change is unconditional; the lookup mismatch will reproduce on every run with this test binary. - **Suggested Fix:** In `testSyncState` (and any other consumer of `StoredEventValues`), replace `values["notification"]` with a lookup that iterates over the map looking for a value whose key ends in `CLOCK_REALTIME` (or reconstruct the expected resource path), OR revert the `createStoredEvent` key strategy for notification values to keep `string(v.DataType)` as the key and use a qualified key only for `METRIC` values where collision is the actual concern. **Suite Issue Class:** test_regression **Suite Severity:** HIGH ## dualnicbc Now I have enough to write the analysis. --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single dualnicbc failure is the parallel "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" test timing out after ~364 seconds because the OS clock never reported a LOCKED state event during the test window, a pattern shared identically across all six parallel event-based suites in this run. **Root Cause Groups:** - additionally, investigate whether the cloud-event-proxy consumer pod on `release-4.22` images reliably emits `OsClockSyncStateChange: LOCKED` log lines within 6 minutes of test start. **Suite Issue Class:** test_flake **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## oc --- ### Suite: oc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The `[oc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` test always times out because a key-naming change in the event parsing library broke how the test reads clock state from received events. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: StoredEventValues map key change breaks `testSyncState()`** (affects: `[It] [oc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** ``` # From test_results_oc.xml (fetched from GCS): <failure message="OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out" type="failed"> [FAILED] OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out In [It] at: /tmp/cnf-v6YRb/ptp-operator-conformance-test/test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:393 @ 07/23/26 11:10:38.94 # Received event contained value="LOCKED" in the raw CloudEvent data: DEBUG [event.go: 730] Context Attributes, ... This is a change to the `main`-branch test code; the `release-4.22` production images are not implicated. (`test/conformance/serial/ptp.go` was also updated in the same commit with 397 lines changed, but `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` was missed.) - **Is it a flake?** No — the key mismatch is deterministic; every run of this test will produce the same timeout. - **Suggested Fix:** In `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:testSyncState()` (lines 413–415), update the event-value lookup to use the ResourceAddress key (e.g. the first key in the `StoredEventValues` map, or a named constant matching the address format), **or** revert the `createStoredEvent()` change for the `data_type`-keyed path. --- ## Cross-Suite Summary — `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-5.0-e2e-telco5g-ptp` **TL;DR:** Hardware sync failures across RDU2 nodes and a deterministic PMC split-args code bug together account for 36 test failures spanning all 7 suites. --- ### Root Cause Groups | # | Description | Count | Suites | |---|-------------|-------|--------| | 1 | **T-GM ts2phc stuck in FREERUN on cnfdf32** — DPLL shows LOCKED but ts2phc reports FREERUN; daemon gate blocks phc2sys, clockClass never reaches 6, cascading to event/metric/holdover failures | **11 / 36** | tgm | | 2 | **BC slave port `ens2f0np0` / hardware clock sync degradation** — BC slave stuck in MASTER state; ptp4l clockClass stuck at 248 (FREERUN) on dualnicbc nodes; cascading to config, event-consumer, and interface-recovery tests | **~14 / 36** | dualnicbc (5–6), dualnicbcha (8–9) | | 3 | **PMC split-args bug in `getClockIDViaPMC`** — commits `3b93c9f408` / `d20ffcbf0c` pass `"GET"`, `"PARENT_DATA_SET"` as two separate argv elements; pmc rejects both with `bad command`; all `ClockSync` tests time out deterministically | **~5 / 36** | dualnicbc (3), dualnicbcha (2) | | 4 | **Parallel event soak test timeout (OCPBUGS-60570 interaction)** — `PushInitialEvent` retry loop (up to 30 × 10 s = 300 s) shares the same 6-minute soak timer; release-5.0 image fix (OCPBUGS-60570) slows initial event delivery, exhausting the budget before LOCKED is received; all 5 parallel suites hit this simultaneously pointing to a cluster-wide event delivery slowdown | **5 / 36** | bc (1), dualfollower (1), oc (1), dualnicbc (1), dualnicbcha (1) | | 5 | **No T-BC topology on cluster** — `CreatePtpConfigurationsWithRetry` finds 0 T-BC L2 solutions on cnfdf30–32; `execute.BeforeAll` calls `Fail()` instead of `Skip()`, failing the first spec and silently skipping the rest | **1 / 36** | tbc | --- ### Cross-Suite Patterns - **Hardware sync failure is the dominant cross-suite thread.** Root causes 1 and 2 both point to the RDU2 test nodes (cnfdf30–32) failing to maintain a healthy PTP grandmaster-to-slave chain during this job window. tgm (ts2phc FREERUN on the GM node) and dualnicbc/dualnicbcha (clockClass/port-state failures on BC nodes) are facets of the same degraded physical sync fabric. - **The PMC split-args bug (root cause 3) is strictly test-code and suite-independent.** It affects every suite that exercises the `getClockIDViaPMC` fallback path — currently triggered only in dualnicbc and dualnicbcha because their log format misses the primary log-parsing path. bc-serial escapes because it locates the clock ID via log parsing before reaching the broken fallback. - **The parallel soak timeout (root cause 4) is cross-suite by construction.** All five parallel sub-suites (`bc-parallel`, `dualfollower-parallel`, `dualnicbc-parallel`, `dualnicbcha-parallel`, `oc-parallel`) run the same `testSyncState` test against the same cluster-event infrastructure. Their simultaneous failure at ≈363 seconds is a single shared event, not five independent regressions. - **Suite-specific failures:** tbc's BeforeAll topology failure is isolated to that suite; tgm's ts2phc FREERUN affects only the GM node tests. --- ### Actionable Recommendations 1. **Fix the PMC split-args bug immediately (highest leverage, deterministic, trivially patched).** In `test/pkg/ptphelper/ptphelper.go` (around line 144), change: ```go // current (broken): []string{"pmc", "-b", "0", "-u", "-f", configFile, "GET", "PARENT_DATA_SET"} // fix: []string{"pmc", "-b", "0", "-u", "-f", configFile, "GET PARENT_DATA_SET"} ``` This matches the existing pattern in `ptptesthelper.go:588` (`GetClockClassViaPMC`) and will immediately unblock ~5 deterministically failing tests in dualnicbc and dualnicbcha. 2. **Investigate and remediate the RDU2 hardware sync chain (cnfdf30–32).** Both the ts2phc FREERUN (tgm) and the BC slave MASTER-state / clockClass-248 failures (dualnicbc, dualnicbcha) trace to the physical PTP grandmaster-to-slave path being degraded during this run window. Check GNSS/GPS signal quality on the GM node, verify the T-GM NIC (ens7fx/WPC card) cable and SFP state, and confirm DPLL lock propagation from GM to BC nodes before re-running. 3. **Decouple `PushInitialEvent` retries from the soak timer, and add a `Skip` path for missing topologies.** For root cause 4: run the `PushInitialEvent` retry loop before starting `tcEndChan`, or extend the parallel soak window to accommodate the OCPBUGS-60570 slow-start behavior. For root cause 5 (tbc): replace the `Fail("Could not create a ptp config…")` call in `execute.BeforeAll` with a `Skip(...)` when no topology is found, preventing misleading failures when the cluster lacks T-BC hardware. --- ### Overall Issue Class `infra_other` + `test_regression` (mixed: ~21 failures are hardware/infra-driven; Job: `Container test exited with code 1, reason Error` at 05:21:35Z (ci-operator-build-log.txt). - **Root Cause:** The test `testSyncState()` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` subscribes to `OsClockSyncStateChange` events and fails at line 393 with `Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out")` when the test-case duration timer fires before the clock ever reports a LOCKED state. The test ran for ~363 seconds without ever receiving a `LOCKED` event. The simultaneous failure of the same event-based test across all 5 parallel suites (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc), plus serial PTP-sync failures in dualnicbc/dualnicbcha/tgm/tbc suites in the same run, points to the PTP grandmaster-to-slave synchronization chain being degraded or disconnected on the RDU2 test hardware during this job window rather than any code regression. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code change identified; this is a hardware/infrastructure timing failure. Test code on `main` already includes commit `4aa5310` (`ci: fix timeout onPushInitialEvent`) which extended `PushInitialEvent` retries to 30×10s for exactly this class of slow-start scenario. The `release-5.0` images are built from `release-5.0` branch. No new cherry-pick or `release-5.0` code change identified as a trigger. - **Is it a flake?** yes — simultaneous failure across 5 independent parallel suites on the same hardware node pool at the same wall-clock time is the hallmark of a transient infra condition (PTP GM link loss, NIC reset, or environmental timing glitch), not a reproducible code defect. The dualfollower serial suite's configuration-verification tests all passed in the same run. - **Suggested Fix:** Re-run the job to confirm this is transient. If it recurs, check whether the PTP grandmaster at `cnfdf30`/`cnfdf31`/`cnfdf32` (RDU2 telco5g lab) was in holdover or FREERUN during the test window by examining the `must-gather` artifacts and `pod-logs` collected at post-step; longer-term, the test could be made more resilient by retrying the entire soak test when zero LOCKED events are observed within the first few minutes. **Suite Issue Class:** test_flake **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## dualnicbc --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (11 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** A split-args bug in the newly introduced `getClockIDViaPMC` helper causes PMC commands to be rejected by the pmc binary, timing out all ClockSync tests; cascading config disruption and an event-consumer timeout account for the remaining failures. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: PMC split-args bug in `getClockIDViaPMC`** (affects: *Slave can sync to master*, *Downstream slave can sync to BC master*, *Can provide a profile with higher priority*) - **Evidence:** ``` parentPortIdentity.clockIdentity not found in pmc output for /var/run/ptp4l.1.config: bad command: GET bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET grandmasterIdentity not found in pmc output for /var/run/ptp4l.1.config: bad command: GET bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET Timed out after 203.731s. / 203.074s. / 201.562s. ``` - **Root Cause:** `getClockIDViaPMC` in `test/pkg/ptphelper/ptphelper.go` (introduced in commit `d20ffcbf0c`, merged via `855c9e1493 2026-07-07`) passes the pmc batch command as **two separate** slice elements — `"GET"` and `"PARENT_DATA_SET"` — rather than the single combined string `"GET PARENT_DATA_SET"`: ```go // BUGGY (ptphelper.go:144): []string{"pmc", "-b", "0", "-u", "-f", configFile, "GET", "PARENT_DATA_SET"} // CORRECT (ptptesthelper.go:588, same repo): []string{"pmc", "-b", "0", "-u", "-f", configFile, "GET PARENT_DATA_SET"} ``` The linuxptp `pmc` binary processes each remaining argv element as a complete batch command. Receiving `"GET"` alone produces `bad command: GET`; `"PARENT_DATA_SET"` alone produces `bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET`. The `getClockIDViaPMC` fallback (reached when log-regex parsing misses the dualnicbc log format) therefore always fails, and the `Eventually` wrapper (3-minute timeout) exhausts before returning, failing these tests. bc-serial passes the equivalent tests because it finds the clock ID via log parsing and never reaches the broken pmc fallback. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** `d20ffcbf0c` / merged `855c9e1493` — _"CNF-19588: CI test for clockClass recovery after cloud-event-proxy crash"_ — `test/pkg/ptphelper/ptphelper.go` — [https://github.com/openshift/ptp-operator/commit/855c9e1493](https://github.com/openshift/ptp-operator/commit/855c9e1493) - **Is it a flake?** No — the split-args bug produces `bad command` on every invocation, deterministically. - **Suggested Fix:** Merge the two separate args into one in `ptphelper.go`: change `"GET", "PARENT_DATA_SET"` to `"GET PARENT_DATA_SET"` as a single slice element, consistent with how `GetClockClassViaPMC` in `ptptesthelper.go:588` already invokes pmc. --- - **Group 2: ptp4l clock class stuck at FREERUN (248)** (affects: *Verify clockClass when locking PTP source on dual NIC boundary clock*, *Should recover clockClass via event API after cloud-event-proxy crash*, *BC clock class recovers to Locked after upstream link outage*) - **Evidence:** ``` Timed out after 300.000s. Expected PMC clock class 6 for /var/run/ptp4l.1.config Expected <int>: 248 to equal <int>: 6 Timed out after 300.000s. Expected ptp4l clock class to eventually be 6 for GM Expected <bool>: false to be true ``` - **Root Cause:** By the time these tests run (>670 s into the suite), test 3 ("Can provide a profile with higher priority") has timed out at 261 s while the temporary high-priority `PtpConfig "temp"` was still active. Although `DeferCleanup(RestorePtp4lConf)` fires, the ensuing ptp4l restart on the dualnicbc node means the second ptp4l instance (`ptp4l.1`) must re-acquire lock. The clockClass tests' 5-minute `Eventually` windows overlap with this re-sync period. Time metrics are not detected ``` - **Root Cause:** With `ptp4l.1` in FREERUN (clock class 248, no upstream lock — see Group 2), the port that should be SLAVE (`ens2f0np0`) remains in MASTER role because ptp4l acts as a grandmaster candidate. `phc2sys` does not start when ptp4l is unlocked (process status=0). PTP time-sync metrics (`openshift_ptp_*`) are not emitted without a locked clock state. All three symptoms are downstream of the same unlocked ptp4l.1 state, compounded by the config disruption from Group 1/2. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Same cascade from `855c9e1493`. No independent breaking commit identified. - **Is it a flake?** No — deterministic cascade. - **Suggested Fix:** Resolving Group 1 and ensuring clean state restore between tests (Group 2) should clear these failures. --- - **Group 4: Event consumer pod log deadline** (affects: *Should receive events after linuxptp-daemon pod restart*, *[dualnicbc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync*) - **Evidence:** ``` getCurrentState did not return SyncStateChange error reading logs in ns=ptp-event-framework-test pod=consumer: context deadline exceeded could not push initial event, err=error reading logs in ns=ptp-event-framework-test pod=consumer: context deadline exceeded ``` - **Root Cause:** Both the serial and parallel event tests fail because the `consumer` pod in namespace `ptp-event-framework-test` does not emit logs within the deadline. The clock hardware was actually LOCKED (confirmed by the raw CloudEvent data), but the test could not detect it and timed out after 5 minutes per `{Enable:true FailureThreshold:1 Duration:5}` config. Full analysis
--- **TL;DR:** A test-code key collision in `createStoredEvent` (commit `d20ffcbf0c`, 2026-07-03) causes every "PTP Slave Clock Sync" event test to miss the LOCKED state and time out. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **15/15 failures: Key collision in `createStoredEvent` silently drops LOCKED notification state, causing all PTP Slave Clock Sync tests to time out** — commit `d20ffcbf0c` on `k8snetworkplumbingwg/ptp-operator` main (merged 2026-07-03) changed the `StoredEventValues` map key from `v.DataType` to `v.Resource` (ResourceAddress). For `os-clock-sync-state-change` events, both the `notification` ("LOCKED") and `metric` (-2) DataValues share the same ResourceAddress (`/cluster/node/.../CLOCK_REALTIME`), so the metric entry silently overwrites the notification entry. `ptp.go:414` then reads `values["notification"]` as `""` (key absent), `wasLocked` never becomes `true`, and the test times out after 5 minutes. The clock hardware was confirmed LOCKED in raw CloudEvent data — this is a **test-code regression only**; production `release-4.22` images are unaffected. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** - **All suites share the same root cause** (`bc`, `oc`, and per the oc analysis also `dualfollower`, `dualnicbc`, `dualnicbcha`). The failure is systematic and code-level: every suite running `Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` hits the same bad key lookup path in `testSyncState`. No suite-specific variation is present — the `clockOffset=0.000000` debug output (instead of the expected metric value) appears in both analyzed suites identically, confirming the key-mismatch affects both the `notification` and `metric` reads. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Revert the map key change in `createStoredEvent`** (`test/pkg/event/event.go`, v2 path, ~line 730): restore `v.DataType` as the key for each DataValue loop iteration, undoing the `d20ffcbf0c` change. This directly fixes all 15 failures across every suite. 2. **Add a regression test for `createStoredEvent`** that asserts the returned map contains both `"notification"` and `"metric"` keys when given a dual-DataValue `os-clock-sync-state-change` event — preventing future key changes from silently breaking event parsing. 3. **Block the PR that introduced `d20ffcbf0c`** (or open a revert PR) against `k8snetworkplumbingwg/ptp-operator` main immediately, as every nightly run on this job will continue to fail 100% of the time until the fix lands. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` **Overall Severity:** HIGH — all 15 test failures trace to a single deterministic code regression on main; no hardware or infra issues involved, but the entire nightly job is broken on every run until resolved. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single bc-parallel test "PTP Slave Clock Sync" timed out unable to detect a LOCKED OS clock because a test-code change in `k8snetworkplumbingwg/ptp-operator` commit `d20ffcbf0c` (July 3, 2026) introduced a key collision bug in `createStoredEvent` that silently drops the notification state from received CloudEvents. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: Key collision in `createStoredEvent` drops LOCKED notification state** (affects: `[It] [bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** ``` [event.go: 730] Context Attributes, type: event.sync.sync-status.os-clock-sync-state-change source: /sync/sync-status/os-clock-sync-state Data, {"version":"1.0","values":[ {"ResourceAddress":"/cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/CLOCK_REALTIME","data_type":"notification","value_type":"enumeration","value":"LOCKED"}, {"ResourceAddress":"/cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/CLOCK_REALTIME","data_type":"metric","value_type":"decimal64.3","value":"-2"} ]} [event.go: 734] Latency for the event: 36942 ms [ptp.go: 411] got map[eventsource:/sync/sync-status/os-clock-sync-state ... eventvalues:map[/cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/CLOCK_REALTIME:-2]] [ptp.go: 419] clockOffset=0.000000 [ptp.go: 393] [FAILED] OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` - **Root Cause:** Commit `d20ffcbf0c` on `k8snetworkplumbingwg/ptp-operator` (merged 2026-07-03, title: "CNF-19588: CI test for clockClass recovery after cloud-event-proxy crash") changed `createStoredEvent` in `test/pkg/event/event.go` (v2 path, line 730) to use `v.Resource` (ResourceAddress) as the `StoredEventValues` map key instead of `v.DataType`. For `os-clock-sync-state-change` events, both DataValues — the `notification` ("LOCKED") and the `metric` (-2) — share the **same** ResourceAddress (`/cluster/node/.../CLOCK_REALTIME`), so the second loop iteration (`metric`) silently overwrites the first (`notification`). The resulting map has no `"notification"` key, so `testSyncState` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:413–414` always reads `state = ""` and `wasLocked` never becomes `true`. The clock hardware was actually LOCKED (confirmed by the raw CloudEvent data), but the test could not detect it and timed out after 5 minutes per `{Enable:true FailureThreshold:1 Duration:5}` config. This is a **test-code regression on main** — the production `release-4.22` images are unaffected. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** `d20ffcbf0c` on `k8snetworkplumbingwg/ptp-operator` main (2026-07-03) — https://github.com/k8snetworkplumbingwg/ptp-operator/commit/d20ffcbf0c - **Is it a flake?** No — the key collision is deterministic and will fail on every run until fixed. - **Suggested Fix:** In `test/pkg/event/event.go` `createStoredEvent` v2 path, restore `v.DataType` as the map key for each DataValue (reverting the key change from `d20ffcbf0c`). To also support the multiple-`metric` BC clock-class use case that motivated the change, either accumulate values into a slice or use a composite key like `"<ResourceAddress>/<DataType>"` and update all callers that read `values["notification"]` and `values["metric"]` accordingly. **Suite Issue Class:** test_regression **Suite Severity:** HIGH ## oc --- ### Suite: oc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single `[oc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` test timed out because the OS clock LOCKED state event was received but its values were keyed by `ResourceAddress` (e.g. `/cluster/node/cnfdf30.../CLOCK_REALTIME:15`) instead of by `DataType` (`"notification"`), so `ptp.go:414` never resolved `state == "LOCKED"` and `wasLocked` remained `false` until the 5-minute timeout fired. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: Event value key mismatch — OS clock LOCKED state never recognized** (affects: `[It] [oc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** ``` [FAILED] OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out In [It] at: /tmp/cnf-s7FwM/ptp-operator-conformance-test/test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:393 @ 07/12/26 11:01:22.395 ``` ``` DEBUG [ptp.go: 411] got map[eventsource:/sync/sync-status/os-clock-sync-state eventtimestamp:2026-07-12 10:55:50.49315292 +0000 UTC eventtype:event.sync.sync-status.os-clock-sync-state-change eventvalues:map[/cluster/node/cnfdf30.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/CLOCK_REALTIME:15]] DEBUG [ptp.go: 419] clockOffset=0.000000 ``` ``` INFO [event.go: 734] Latency for the event: 36928 ms INFO [event.go: 589] received term signal, exiting MonitorPodLogsRegex ``` The `SynchronizedBeforeSuite` also shows: `PtpEventsIsConsumerReady= false` at start, confirming the consumer app was only ready after the 1-minute `BeforeEach` warmup. - **Root Cause:** `testSyncState` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` subscribes to `OsClockSyncStateChange` events and calls `event.PushInitialEvent` to get the current clock state. The initial event IS received (a stale LOCKED event timestamped 37 seconds earlier), but its `StoredEventValues` map uses the `ResourceAddress` path (`/cluster/node/cnfdf30.../CLOCK_REA

Commit `d20ffcb` (CNF-19588, Jul 3 2026) changed `createStoredEvent` in `test/pkg/event/event.go` to key the values map by `v.Resource` (ResourceAddress, e.g., `/cluster/node/cnfdf32.../CLOCK_REALTIME`) instead of `v.DataType` (e.g., `"notification"`). --- ## Cross-Suite Summary — `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-4.22-e2e-telco5g-ptp` --- **TL;DR:** linuxptp-daemon PR #618 broke T-GM holdover and T-BC sync (9 failures); a test-code key regression in `d20ffcb` broke all 5 parallel event tests (5 failures). --- ### Root Cause Groups | # | Failures | Suites | Root Cause | |---|----------|--------|------------| | 1 | **9 / 15** | tbc (×6), tgm (×3) | `openshift/linuxptp-daemon` **PR #618** (`d2ff2d154d`, merged `release-4.22` 2026-06-30): T-GM holdover state machine bypassed — class 7 never emitted, jumps LOCKED (6) → FREERUN (248) directly; T-BC on `cnfdf32` consequently stuck in FREERUN (class 248) with phc2sys never starting, cascading to all 6 tbc serial tests. | | 2 | **5 / 15** | bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc (all `-parallel`) | Test-code regression **commit `d20ffcb`** (Jul 3, 2026, `main`): `createStoredEvent` in `test/pkg/event/event.go` changed the values-map key from `DataType` (`"notification"`) to `ResourceAddress`; `testSyncState()` still looks up `values["notification"]`, always gets nil, `wasLocked` never becomes `true`. All 5 parallel suites ran concurrently, all timed out at ≈363 s (`ptp.go:393`). Secondary contributor: `cloud-event-proxy:release-4.22` sidecar and/or the shared `consumer` pod in `ptp-event-framework-test` may not have been serving `OsClockSyncStateChange` events during the parallel window (`PushInitialEvent` exhausted all 30 × 10 s retries). | | 3 | **1 / 15** | dualnicbcha (serial) | phc2sys HA failover — phc2sys did not switch to secondary ptp4l after the primary interface was downed, or failed to emit the expected log line. Likely a cascade of the T-BC FREERUN state from Group 1. | --- ### Cross-Suite Patterns - **tbc + tgm share one root cause** (PR #618): T-GM FREERUN propagates upstream — T-BC has no grandmaster to lock to, leaving cnfdf32 in class 248 and making all T-BC serial tests deterministically fail. Fixing PR #618 unblocks both suites. - **All 5 parallel suites share one root cause** (commit `d20ffcb`): bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, and oc fail with identical timing (≈363 s) and identical failure message, confirming a single shared code path broken by the same commit. - **dualnicbcha is the only suite with failures from two separate groups** (1 parallel event + 1 serial HA), making it appear to have the most diverse failure set. - **No suite-specific flakes** — every failure traces to one of the two breaking changes above. --- ### Actionable Recommendations 1. **Revert or patch `openshift/linuxptp-daemon` PR #618 on `release-4.22`** — restore the T-GM holdover servo-state path so ts2phc emits clock class 7 before transitioning to 248, and re-add the FREERUN `ptp-state-change` CloudEvent dispatch in the restarted-ts2phc code path. --- **TL;DR:** 11/13 failures are a test/daemon version mismatch; 2/13 are a T-GM hardware holdover regression. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **11/13 failures — `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` log line absent from `release-4.20` daemon** (bc: 3, dualfollower: 3, dualnicbc: 3, dualnicbcha: 2) The test helper `GetProfileLogID` (test code running from `main`) searches for a log regex (`Ptp4lConf: #profile: <name>`) that is only emitted after `*configInput = configOutput` + `printNodeProfile` — code introduced in linuxptp-daemon `main` PR #617 (commit `fd6ff1e`, merged 2026-06-30) and **not backported to `release-4.20`**. The CI job deploys `release-4.20` daemon images (`USE_UPSTREAM=false`), so the pattern is never logged. `GetProfileLogID`'s internal 3-minute deadline consumes the entire outer `Eventually` window, leaving the `/var/run` fallback no time to run. All failures surface as either `timedout waiting for matches` (stream alive, pattern never appeared) or `context deadline exceeded` (Kubernetes log-stream dropped), with a secondary cluster-API `Unauthorized` state observed post-run in dualnicbc/dualnicbcha, consistent with infrastructure stress from 11 concurrent 200-second stream-read timeouts. - **2/13 failures — T-GM DPLL does not enter holdover on GNSS disruption** (tgm: 2) When GNSS signal is lost or rebooted on WPC T-GM hardware, the Intel DPLL is expected to transition to `lockStatus: holdover` (clock class 7). Instead, the DPLL stays in or falls directly to `FREERUN` (clock class 248), so `openshift_ptp_clock_class` never reaches 7. Both tgm tests fail: one by timeout polling for clock class 7, one by direct metric assertion finding 248. This is independent of the log-line issue; the `release-4.20` daemon or underlying firmware may have a regression in holdover state management. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Root Cause | Suites Affected | Failures | |---|---|---| | `#profile:` log line missing from `release-4.20` daemon | bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha | 11 | | T-GM DPLL does not enter holdover (clock class 7) | tgm | 2 | The 11-failure group is fully cross-suite: all four PTP topology suites hit identical pod-log-stream errors on six different pods across different nodes, ruling out any single pod, node, or suite-specific cause. The tgm failures are completely isolated to hardware GNSS/holdover behavior and share no mechanism with the other 11. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Backport linuxptp-daemon `main` PR #617 to `release-4.20`** (fixes 11/13 failures). The commit `fd6ff1e` adds `*configInput = configOutput` and the unconditional `printNodeProfile` call that emits `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` into daemon logs. Without this, any `main` test branch that relies on `GetProfileLogID` will always time out against `release-4.20` images. Alternatively, gate the test's log-search path on daemon version and immediately fall through to the `/var/run` exec-based fallback instead of waiting a full 3 minutes first. 2. **Investigate T-GM DPLL holdover regression on `release-4.20`** (fixes 2/13 failures). Audit recent commits to `linuxptp-daemon/release-4.20` and `cloud-event-proxy/release-4.20` for changes in `pkg/event/event.go` DPLL state-transition logic. Also verify WPC hardware firmware version matches the expected holdover capability; if firmware is the variable, pin firmware version in the CI telco5g cluster. 3. **Extend the outer `Eventually` timeout in `BasicClockSyncCheck`** or make the `/var/run` fallback the *first* path (not the last resort). The current design serially exhausts a 3-minute log-stream deadline before trying a fast exec-based config read, meaning any transient log-streaming disruption (or version mismatch) guarantees test failure regardless of actual PTP sync state. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` (The dominant failure mode — 11/13 — is caused by `main` test code depending on a daemon log line that exists only in `main`, not in the `release-4.20` images the job is actually testing. The tgm pair is `test_failure` / potential daemon regression, but does not change the overall classification.) **Overall Severity:** HIGH (11/13 failures are systemic and reproducible across every topology suite in every run that pairs `main` test scripts with `release-4.20` daemon images; Based on the suite analyses in the prompt, here is the cross-suite summary: --- **Job:** `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-5.0-e2e-telco5g-ptp-upstream` **Version:** 5.0 | **Total failures:** 38 --- ## TL;DR Two code regressions merged in late June broke metric label lookups and T-BC topology discovery, cascading to 38 failures. --- ## Root Cause Groups - **~36/38 failures — Interface alias mismatch in `openshift_ptp_clock_state` metric labels** (primary driver across dualfollower and likely all remaining suites) Upstream PR #617 (`k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon`, commit `fd6ff1e`, merged 2026-06-30) introduced `pkg/alias/manager.go`, causing `updateClockStateMetrics()` to emit `iface="ens2fx"` (NIC alias) instead of `iface="ens2f0np0"` (raw interface name). All conformance tests that look up `openshift_ptp_clock_state` by raw interface name now find nothing, producing deterministic `<iface> not found` failures. - **2/38 failures — T-BC topology solver finds no valid WPC NIC configuration** (tbc + all suites) PR #205 (`openshift/ptp-operator`, commit `84723c8`/`aede1e9`, merged 2026-06-23) added `PtpConfigTelcoBC()` with strict solver constraints requiring WPC NICs (Intel E810 with GNSS/PPS) as both T-BC receiver and local grandmaster. The `cnfdf30/31/32` lab hardware satisfies none of these constraints, so `[SynchronizedBeforeSuite]` aborts before any test body runs. --- ## Cross-Suite Patterns | Root cause | dualfollower | tbc | all | Notes | |---|---|---|---|---| | Interface alias mismatch (`fd6ff1e`) | Yes (4 explicit) | Likely | Likely | Pervasive — any test querying `openshift_ptp_clock_state` by raw `iface` label fails | | T-BC topology absent | No | Yes (1) | Yes (1) | Suite-specific to TBC tests; `SynchronizedBeforeSuite` abort means no TBC test can pass regardless | The interface alias regression is cross-suite because `openshift_ptp_clock_state` metric lookups by raw interface name are used throughout ptp-operator conformance tests. The T-BC issue is isolated to suites that exercise the `AlgoTelcoBCString` solver path. --- ## Actionable Recommendations 1. **Fix metric label lookup in ptp-operator tests (unblocks ~36 failures).** In the conformance test helper that queries `openshift_ptp_clock_state`, resolve the slave interface name through the same alias lookup used by the daemon (`alias.GetAliasValue(ifname)`) before comparing metric labels. Alternatively, the linuxptp-daemon can expose both raw and alias labels, or revert the alias-in-metric-label behavior in PR #617 pending a coordinated test update. 2. **Skip T-BC suite gracefully when hardware is absent (unblocks 2 failures).** Change `PtpConfigTelcoBC()` (and the `[SynchronizedBeforeSuite]` that calls it) to emit a Ginkgo `Skip()` rather than a hard `Expect(...).To(BeNil())` error when `solutions["TBC"]` is empty. This prevents a missing hardware topology from failing the entire suite and turning an expected "not applicable" into a false failure. 3. **Gate the T-BC test job on lab hardware capabilities.** Add a pre-flight check (or CI job label) that confirms at least one `cnfdfXX` node has WPC NIC interfaces before running TBC tests, and skip or xfail the job if the hardware inventory is insufficient. --- ## Overall Issue Class `test_regression` ## Overall Severity **HIGH** — Two deterministic regressions introduced within one week of each other account for all 38 failures; no flakes involved. The interface alias change blocks the majority of PTP conformance coverage until tests or daemon are updated. --- # Per-Suite Details ## dualfollower --- ### Suite: dualfollower (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 dualfollower tests fail because a June 30 upstream sync PR introduced interface aliasing in linuxptp-daemon that changes `openshift_ptp_clock_state` metric labels from raw interface names (e.g., `ens2f0np0`) to NIC-level aliases (e.g., `ens2fx`), while the test code still queries raw names. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: Interface alias mismatch in `openshift_ptp_clock_state` metric** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Dual follower can sync when one follower port goes down", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` no Follower port in expected LOCKED state for ptpconfig test-slave1 (node=cnfdf31.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com, label="ptp/clock-under-test", slaveIfs=[ens2f0np0 ens2f3np3]). This is on the upstream `main` branch that the CI job builds directly (`git clone --single-branch --branch main https://github.com/k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon.git`); the `release-5.0` backport check is not applicable since the daemon image itself is built from upstream `main`, not from a `release-5.0` branch. - **Is it a flake?** No — all three serial tests fail with identical, deterministic `ens2f0np0 not found` errors; this is a code change, not environmental noise. - **Suggested Fix:** Update the ptp-operator conformance test helper that looks up `openshift_ptp_clock_state` metrics to resolve the slave interface name through `GetAliasValue()` (or the same alias lookup the daemon uses) before querying Prometheus, so tests use `ens2fx` instead of `ens2f0np0`. Alternatively, add a NIC-alias-aware lookup path in `ptptesthelper.go`. - **Group 2: OS Clock never reached LOCKED (event-based parallel test)** (affects: "PTP Slave Clock Sync" [dualfollower-parallel]) - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` - **Root Cause:** The parallel event-based test monitors OS clock LOCKED state via the cloud-event-proxy event stream. Because the upstream alias change makes ptp4l metrics unreliable for the dual-follower node (and may affect how phc2sys status is inferred by cloud-event-proxy), the LOCKED event is never published. This is a cascade from Group 1's alias mismatch, combined with the fact that the parallel test ran concurrently with the serial tests that were also perturbing the dual-follower configuration on the same node. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Same as Group 1 — `fd6ff1e` in `k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon`. - **Is it a flake?** No — the root event-system failure is deterministic given the broken metrics; however, the exact timing coupling with serial tests adds some environmental component. - **Suggested Fix:** Fix Group 1 first (alias-aware metric lookup); if the OS clock lock event still doesn't fire, check whether cloud-event-proxy also reads `openshift_ptp_clock_state` by raw inte
