Full analysis
--- **TL;DR:** All 23 failures trace to T-GM node `cnfdf32` stuck in FREERUN (clockClass 248), collapsing PTP sync cluster-wide. **Root Cause Groups:** - **22/23 failures — T-GM GNSS loss / FREERUN (clockClass 248) cascading to all PTP consumers:** `cnfdf32` never achieved or held GNSS lock (node was only 24 minutes old at tgm suite start; DPLL went directly to FREERUN rather than HOLDOVER on signal loss). With no locked grandmaster, all downstream boundary clocks and slaves had no synchronization source. This manifested as: - Log-stream timeouts (`timedout waiting for matches`, `context deadline exceeded`) across dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc — because `linuxptp-daemon` never emitted the sync-state lines tests poll for. - `OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out` in parallel event tests across four suites. - `Expected PMC clock class 6 … got 248` in tbc. - `Timed out waiting for clock class 7 / state FREERUN and ClockClass 248` in all 3 tgm serial tests. Affected pods: `linuxptp-daemon-qtq2t`, `k7dlc`, `2klsv`, `gvdmf`, `5zjch`, `htnss`, `49flz` — every daemon pod in the cluster. - **1/23 failures — Kubernetes API server degradation (dualfollower log-stream exhaustion variant):** By approximately 06:22 UTC the API server began returning `Unauthorized` / credential errors, causing `GetConfigForProfileFromVarRun` fallback exec calls to fail and the test runner process to be force-killed (`process timed out`, `exit status 127`). This is a secondary symptom of the extended infra instability in the same run. **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Suite | Failures | Root Cause | |---|---|---| | dualfollower | 4/4 | FREERUN cascade + API server degradation | | dualnicbc | 4/4 | FREERUN cascade | | dualnicbcha | 4/4 | FREERUN cascade | | tbc | 7/8 | FREERUN cascade; 1 separate FIFO scheduling assertion | | tgm | 3/3 | GNSS/DPLL never entered HOLDOVER (root cause node) | All five suites share the identical FREERUN cascade. The tgm suite is unique in that it directly tests the T-GM hardware — the failures there are the _origin_, not a downstream symptom. The tbc suite has one anomalous failure (FIFO scheduling assertion mismatch) that is suite-specific and not shared with others. **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Add a GNSS lock readiness gate before tgm (and the full test phase):** Verify `ClockClass 6` and `nmea_status 1` stable for ≥5 minutes on `cnfdf32` before advancing past cluster setup. The 24-minute node age at test start is a clear signal the GNSS receiver had not converged. 2. **Retry the job with cnfdf32 at full lock stability:** This is a transient infra condition (GNSS signal loss / premature test start), not a code regression. No code change on `release-4.20` is implicated. A clean retry with a fully locked T-GM is expected to pass. 3. **Investigate lab GNSS antenna/RF chain on cnfdf32:** If retries repeatedly show FREERUN or short node ages, the antenna connection or signal source on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` (rdu2 lab) may have a hardware fault that needs physical inspection. **Overall Issue Class:** `test_flake` **Overall Severity:** HIGH --- # Per-Suite Details ## dualfollower --- ### Suite: dualfollower (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 failures stem from the test framework being unable to read expected log patterns from the `linuxptp-daemon-container` pod (`linuxptp-daemon-qtq2t`), a symptom present identically across every test suite in this job, indicating a cluster-wide API/log-streaming failure rather than any code regression. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream unreachable / log buffer rolled** (affects: all 3 serial ClockSync tests) - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-qtq2t container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-qtq2t container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` Post-job gather steps confirm API degradation: ``` error: You must be logged in to the server (the server has asked for the client to provide credentials) error getting cluster version: Unauthorized ClusterVersion: Installing "" for <unknown>: <unknown> ``` The test entrypoint was force-killed: ``` {"error":"process timed out",...,"msg":"Process did not exit before 15s grace period"} error: failed to execute wrapped command: exit status 127 ``` - **Root Cause:** The test helper `GetProfileLogID` (`test/pkg/ptphelper/ptphelper.go:84`) streams the `linuxptp-daemon-container` logs looking for the regex `Ptp4lConf: #profile: <name>(.|\n)*?message_tag \[(.*)\]`. This line is only logged once at daemon startup via `printNodeProfile` → `printWhenNotNil(nodeProfile.Ptp4lConf, "Ptp4lConf")` (linuxptp-daemon `pkg/daemon/daemon.go:1521`). The daemon pods started ~2 hours before the dualfollower suite ran (cluster setup completed at 02:37, tests began at 04:13); ptp4l logs at ~1 line/second, so the 10 MB Kubernetes log buffer was fully rotated past the startup `Ptp4lConf:` entry by test time, causing the Pass 1 snapshot search to miss it. Pass 2 (live follow) then timed out (`timedout waiting for matches`) because the profile is never re-logged unless reconciled. The `GetConfigForProfileFromVarRun` fallback (`ptphelper.go:524`, exec into the container to inspect `/var/run`) also failed, consistent with the Kubernetes API server becoming `Unauthorized` / overloaded by ~06:22. The identical error pattern appears across every test suite and 7 distinct daemon pods (qtq2t, k7dlc, 2klsv, gvdmf, 5zjch, htnss, 49flz), which confirms the issue is cluster-wide, not profile-specific or suite-specific. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code regression identified; the `printNodeProfile` log path in `daemon.go:1521` is unchanged in both `main` and `release-4.20`. - **Is it a flake?** No — the failure is consistent across every suite (dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc, tgm) in the same job run, indicating a systemic cluster condition, not a random flake. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the job; if it recurs, investigate whether the Kubernetes API server token expired during the 4-hour run or whether log buffer exhaustion is structural — in the latter case, consider adding periodic `ptpconfig` reconcile triggers or having `GetProfileLogID` issue an exec-based config read (`GetConfigForProfileFromVarRun`) earlier, without waiting the full 3-minute log-stream timeout. - **Group 2: OS Clock never LOCKED** (affects: `[dualfollower-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` - **Root Cause:** PTP clock synchronization requires the upstream ClockSync serial tests (Group 1) to first establish PTP sync state. Because the serial tests could not verify the PTP profile was applied (Group 1 failure), the clock never entered the `LOCKED` state, and the parallel event test timed out waiting for it. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — cascading from Group 1. - **Is it a flake?** No — same cluster-wide condition as Group 1. - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1; no independent fix needed for this test. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_other **Suite Severity:** HIGH ## dualnicbc --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 failures share a single root cause — the Telecom Grandmaster (T-GM) node was stuck in FREERUN (clock class 248, no GNSS lock), causing every downstream PTP clock in the lab to fail to sync, which made all log-scraping and LOCKED-state assertions time out. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: T-GM GNSS/hardware failure cascades to all PTP sync tests** (affects: all 4 dualnicbc failures) - **Evidence:** - From `junit-failures.txt` (dualnicbc serial tests, 3 occurrences): ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-k7dlc container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` - From `junit-failures.txt` (dualnicbc parallel event test): ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` - From `junit-failures.txt` (tgm suite — the upstream hardware): ``` expected a metric [7.000000] but got map[/cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/ens7fx/master:248] ``` Clock class 248 = FREERUN (no GNSS signal); class 6 = GPS-locked. The T-GM node `cnfdf32` is not locked to any grandmaster source. - tgm suite: `Timed out waiting for clock class 7` (holdover) and `Timed out waiting for state FREERUN and ClockClass 248` — confirming the T-GM is unresponsive to GNSS-loss stimuli throughout the entire run. - From `ci-operator-build-log.txt`, all 6 nodes were `Ready` and the cluster was healthy, ruling out OCP infra: ``` [INFO] All 6 nodes are Ready. ``` - The same "timedout waiting for matches" / "never LOCKED" pattern appears identically across **all five** test suites (dualnicbc, dualfollower, dualnicbcha, tbc, tgm), confirming a lab-wide PTP sync failure, not a per-suite regression. - **Root Cause:** The T-GM hardware node `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` (the GNSS-backed grandmaster) was running in FREERUN (clock class 248) for the entire test window — it was not receiving a valid GNSS signal. Without a locked T-GM, the boundary clock master (`test-bc-master1`, pod `linuxptp-daemon-k7dlc`) has no upstream PTP master to synchronize to, so `ptp4l` never outputs a foreign clock ID in its logs. The test framework (`GetProfileName`/`WaitForForeignMaster` in `test/conformance/serial/ptp.go`) polls the container log stream for that output with a ~202s timeout, finds nothing, and fails. The parallel event test's OS clock similarly never transitions from FREE_RUN to LOCKED. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — the only recent linuxptp-daemon commit (`14eb788`, "Sync from upstream 22-Jul-2026") exists only on `main`, not on `release-4.20`. This job builds from `release-4.20` images (`PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.20`), so that commit cannot be the cause. No code change on `release-4.20` is implicated. - **Is it a flake?** Yes — transient GNSS signal loss or lab hardware instability at `cnfdf32` in the rdu2 telco lab. The `investigation-summary.txt` independently assessed this as severity LOW / infra class. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the periodic job; if the T-GM comes back into GPS lock the run should pass. If this recurs across multiple nightly runs, investigate GNSS antenna/receiver health on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` and check whether `ts2phc` is able to read a valid NMEA/PPS signal. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_timeout **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM (all 4 tests fail, but cause is transient hardware/infra, not a code regression) ## dualnicbcha --- ### Suite: dualnicbcha (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 dualnicbcha tests fail because the PTP clock never achieved LOCK state — the `linuxptp-daemon` remained in FREERUN (clockClass 248), so every log-regex poll timed out and the event-based test saw no LOCKED signal. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon never produces clock-sync log lines** (affects: `Slave can sync to master [Serial]`, `Downstream slave can sync to BC master [Serial]`, `DualNICBCHA phc2sys switches to secondary ptp4l when primary interface fails [Serial]`) - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-gvdmf container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-5zjch container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` Cross-suite corroboration from `junit-failures.txt` (tbc suite): ``` Expected PMC clock class 6 for /var/run/ptp4l.0.config Expected <int>: 248 to equal <int>: 6 ``` And from the tgm suite: `expected a metric [7.000000] but got map[...master:248]` - **Root Cause:** The PTP chain never locks: `clockClass 248` (FREERUN) is reported across every suite in this run (dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc, tgm all fail identically). The test framework polls `linuxptp-daemon-container` log streams for foreign-clock-ID regex patterns that only appear once ptp4l achieves sync; because the daemon stays in FREERUN those patterns are never emitted, so the 202 s timeout fires. The two error variants — `context deadline exceeded` (stream exhausted) vs. `timedout waiting for matches` (stream open, no match) — are both symptoms of the same absence of sync-state log lines. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code change identified; failure is uniform across all suites and corresponds to clockClass=248 lab-wide, pointing to a transient PTP hardware/signal issue in the telco5g test bed (cnfdf30–32 nodes, rdu2.redhat.com). - **Is it a flake?** yes - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the job; if the same run-wide FREERUN pattern recurs, investigate GNSS signal and hardware connectivity on the cnfdf30/31/32 telco5g nodes. - **Group 2: Event-based OS Clock never LOCKED** (affects: `Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync [dualnicbcha-parallel]`) - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` (Identical messages in dualfollower-parallel, dualnicbc-parallel, tbc-parallel — all parallel event suites in the same job run.) - **Root Cause:** The event-based test waits for a `LOCKED` clock-state notification from cloud-event-proxy; because the underlying ptp4l/phc2sys never achieved lock (FREERUN across the board, same root cause as Group 1), no LOCKED event is ever published and the test times out. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — same infra root cause as Group 1. - **Is it a flake?** yes - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1 — retry once the test bed PTP signal is restored; the event-bus path is functioning correctly but has no synced clock to report. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_other **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## tbc --- ### Suite: tbc (8 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** 7 of 8 failures stem from the T-GM GNSS signal being lost (clockClass=248/FREERUN), collapsing PTP sync across all downstream nodes; the 8th is a separate test-assertion mismatch on FIFO scheduling. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: T-GM GNSS/infrastructure failure — PTP clock never locked** (affects: "PTP daemon apply match rule based on nodeLabel", "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority", "Check GM announcements in locked mode and holdover transitions", "Should recover clockClass via event API after cloud-event-proxy crash", "PTP Slave Clock Sync [parallel]") - **Evidence:** ``` could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-htnss container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-htnss container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches Expected PMC clock class 6 for /var/run/ptp4l.0.config Expected <int>: 248 to equal <int>: 6 OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` From `junit-failures.txt`, the identical error (`timedout waiting for matches` / `context deadline exceeded`) appears in every other suite — dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha — and from `test_results_tgm.xml`: ``` expected a metric [7.000000] but got map[ /cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/ens7fx/master:248] Timed out waiting for clock class 7 Timed out waiting for state FREERUN and ClockClass 248 ``` - **Root Cause:** The T-GM node (`cnfdf32`, the WPC Grand Master) never acquired GNSS lock — its ptp4l clockClass remained at 248 (holdover expired / FREERUN) instead of the expected 6 (LOCKED). With no locked T-GM, the entire downstream PTP chain (T-BC, T-BC-follower) had no source to synchronise to. As a result, `linuxptp-daemon-container` never emitted the log lines the tests poll for (slave-port selection, profile acquisition, clock-ID announcements), causing log-stream watchers to time out with `context deadline exceeded` or `timedout waiting for matches`. The clockClass-recovery test confirms the signal was never restored: `pmc` read 248 for the entire 300 s window. This failure pattern is identical across all five test suites in this job run, proving it is a cluster-wide hardware/environment condition and not a code regression. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code change is responsible; this is a GNSS/RF hardware or connectivity failure on the test-bed node `cnfdf32`. - **Is it a flake?** Yes — this failure mode (GNSS signal loss causing full-suite collapse) recurs transiently when the test-bed loses GPS signal or antenna connectivity. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the periodic job; if GNSS failures recur, the test-bed team should inspect the GPS antenna/receiver on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` and verify GNSS lock is stable before running PTP conformance tests. - **Group 2: FIFO-scheduling test assertion mismatch** (affects: "Should check whether using fifo scheduling") - **Evidence:** ``` Expected <map[string]int64 | len:2>: { "test-wpc-tbc_tbc-tr": 10, "test-wpc-tbc_tbc-tt": 10, } to have length 0 ``` From `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/hardwareconfig/testdata/tbc-gnrd.yaml`: ```yaml ptpSchedulingPolicy: SCHED_FIFO ptpSchedulingPriority: 10 ``` (present for both `tbc-tt` and `tbc-tr` profiles in the tbc-gnrd config) - **Root Cause:** The `release-4.20` deployed ptpconfig `test-wpc-tbc` has both `tbc-tr` and `tbc-tt` profiles configured with `ptpSchedulingPolicy: SCHED_FIFO` and `ptpSchedulingPriority: 10`. This is a test/production-config mismatch: the test appears to expect the baseline ptpconfig to have no SCHED_FIFO processes, but the release-4.20 tbc config intentionally enables FIFO scheduling on both T-BC profiles. (×32+) → GNSS enabled → [FAILED] Timed out waiting for clock class 7 (ptp.go:3802, 3m20s elapsed) # Test 2 — class 7 transient, immediately overtaken by 248: PtpClockClassChange Event recieved map[metric:6], map[metric:7], map[metric:248] GnssStateChange Event recieved map[metric:0 notification:FAILURE-NOFIX] → STEP: Verifying ClockClass value to 7 → [FAILED] expected a metric [7.000000] but got map[metric:248] (ptp.go:3898) ``` - **Root Cause:** After GNSS signal loss on node `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com`, the WPC hardware DPLL transitions directly from LOCKED to FREERUN (class 248) without sustaining HOLDOVER (class 7). In test 2, HOLDOVER was briefly entered (class 7 visible in the event stream) but exhausted faster than the metric-polling step could verify it — confirming the DPLL holdover window on this hardware is abnormally short. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — the upstream sync PR #613 (`0459bc0`, CNF-24996/OCPBUGS-90708, merged 2026-06-23) was an upstream sync to the main branch and does not directly affect the `release-4.22` branch under test; no code regression identified. - **Is it a flake?** Yes — test 2 shows holdover *does* occur but transiently; this is a hardware-timing sensitivity, consistent with intermittent failures when DPLL holdover duration is at the margins. - **Suggested Fix:** Investigate DPLL holdover configuration on `cnfdf32` (check `LocalHoldoverTimeout` configured for the WPC hardware); if hardware is degraded or misconfigured for holdover, reprovisioning or reconfiguring the node should resolve it. `GetProfileLogID` (`ptphelper.go:69`) uses the regex `(?m).*?Ptp4lConf: #profile: %s(.|\n)*?message_tag \[(.*)\]`. This pattern is only logged by `printNodeProfile` (`daemon.go:893`) when `nodeProfile.Ptp4lConf` already contains the RENDERED config (i.e., after `*configInput = configOutput` at `daemon.go:1119` has updated the pointer's string on a PREVIOUS apply). On the very first profile application, `printNodeProfile` logs the RAW CRD config which has NO `#profile:` header and NO `message_tag` line — both are added only by `RenderPtp4lConf()` / `ExtendGlobalSection()`. Since the daemon applied profiles once at cluster-setup time (~2 hours before tests) and never re-applied them, neither Pass 1 (existing logs) nor Pass 2 (follow stream) in `GetPodLogsRegex` (`pods.go:214`) can find the pattern. Test 3 (higher priority profile) also creates a brand-new `temp` profile that is applied exactly once, so the same structural mismatch applies.

**TL;DR:** 13 of 15 failures are a single test flake — a log-rotation race that voids `GetProfileLogID`; 2 are a WPC T-GM holdover hardware failure. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **13/15 failures — `GetProfileLogID` log-rotation timeout (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc):** `BasicClockSyncCheck` → `GetClockIDForeign` → `GetProfileLogID` (ptphelper.go:69) opens a `Follow: true` log stream and searches for `(?m).*?Ptp4lConf: #profile: <name>(.|\n)*?message_tag \[.*\]`. That regex only matches a line emitted once by `printNodeProfile` at daemon startup. Cluster setup finished at ~02:06; ClockSync tests ran at ~04:00–06:00 (~2–4 h later). By then CRI-O log rotation had cycled the startup entry out of the K8s-served log buffer. The snapshot read yields nothing (~26 s); the follow-stream times out after 180 s — producing the observed ~206–218 s failure duration across every suite. The "Can provide a profile with higher priority" variant adds ~60–90 s of profile-apply wait before hitting the same timeout (yielding ~287–298 s). - **2/15 failures — WPC T-GM DPLL holdover not sustained (tgm):** After GNSS disconnect, ts2phc either never emits the `"holdover"` log token (test 1 — 3+ min of retries, class stays at 6) or emits class-7 followed 7 ms later by class-248 (test 2 — DPLL flips through holdover and drops to FREERUN before the test can assert it). Root is in the WPC DPLL firmware or the `ts2phc_parser.go:162-163` holdover-detection logic, not in the test itself. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Suite | Failures | Root Cause | |---|---|---| | bc | 3 | Log rotation (GetProfileLogID) | | dualfollower | 3 | Log rotation (GetProfileLogID) | | dualnicbc | 3 | Log rotation (GetProfileLogID) | | dualnicbcha | 2 | Log rotation (GetProfileLogID) — same 206–218 s signature | | oc | 2 | Log rotation (GetProfileLogID) — same 206–218 s signature | | tgm | 2 | WPC T-GM holdover hardware/firmware failure | All five non-tgm suites share an identical root cause and near-identical failure durations. The dualnicbcha and oc per-suite analyses characterize the symptom as "LOCKED state timeout," but the timing signature (206–218 s, 287–298 s) and the `GetProfileLogID` call stack are identical to bc/dualfollower/dualnicbc. tgm is the only suite with a distinct, hardware-level root cause. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Fix `GetProfileLogID` to not depend on rotated startup logs.** Instead of parsing the one-shot `printNodeProfile` startup line, query the current PTP configuration state directly — either from a `/metrics` endpoint, the `PtpConfig` CR status, or by triggering a daemon profile-reload (e.g., a no-op annotation update) immediately before the test assertion window. This single fix eliminates 13/15 failures. 2. **Investigate the WPC DPLL holdover firmware behavior on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com`.** Capture `ts2phc` raw log output during GNSS disconnect to determine whether the DPLL hardware never enters holdover or enters and exits it within milliseconds. If the latter, check whether the DPLL holdover duration register is being set correctly from `ts2phc.holdover` config and whether a firmware update is available for the WPC card. 3. **Add a canary assertion for daemon log reachability before ClockSync tests.** A short pre-check that confirms `GetPodLogsRegex` can read at least one log line from the daemon container (not the specific startup pattern) would convert a silent 206 s timeout into a fast, labeled skip/infra-failure, making future regressions easier to triage. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_flake` (13/15); `infra_other` (2/15 tgm) **Overall Severity:** MEDIUM — the dominant failure mode is a pre-existing, deterministic time-dependent flake (not a code regression); the tgm failures indicate a real hardware/firmware problem on one lab node but affect only 2 tests. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (3 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 3 bc ClockSync tests fail at the same step — `GetProfileLogID` cannot find the regex `Ptp4lConf: #profile: <name>...message_tag [...]` in the linuxptp-daemon container logs, because this log pattern only appears on a second profile application and the daemon had been stable for ~2 hours since cluster setup with no re-apply. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: `GetProfileLogID` regex never matches — daemon log pattern requires second profile apply** (affects: all 3 tests) - **Evidence:** ``` [Jun 26 04:03:18.197][ptphelper.go: 123] GetProfileLogID attempt 1 failed for test-bc-master1_test-bc-master1: could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-nqmpf container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded, retrying... In this run, ~2 hours of stability after setup meant no re-apply occurred. - **Suggested Fix:** In `GetProfileLogID`, instead of relying on the `Ptp4lConf:` log (which requires a second apply), read the ptp4l config file directly from the pod filesystem (`/var/run/ptp4l.0.config`)

**TL;DR:** Cluster log-stream collapse + API auth expiry wiped 22 of 24 tests; a linuxptp-daemon state-machine regression caused the remaining 2 TGM holdover failures. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **12/24 failures — linuxptp-daemon log stream unreachable (ClockSync `GetProfileLogID`/`GetClockIDForeign` timeout):** All ClockSync tests in bc (3), dualfollower (3), dualnicbc (3), dualnicbcha (2), and oc-serial test 1 (1) hit `could not find regex in log stream … error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded` or `timedout waiting for matches`. The test helper opens a live `kubectl logs --follow` stream and waits for a ptp4l profile or sync line that never arrives within 3–10 minutes. Two compounding factors: (a) ptp4l may not be reaching SLAVE state cluster-wide (no grandmaster reachable or log format changed by PR #613), and (b) when the API server began degrading, streaming connections were silently severed rather than cleanly refused. - **10/24 failures — Kubernetes API server Unauthorized mid-run (oc-serial tests 2–11):** After the first oc-serial test completed at ~07:29 UTC, the kubeconfig credentials expired or the API server rejected them (`Unauthorized` / `couldn't get current server API group list: the server has asked for the client to provide credentials`). Test 1 exhausted its 3-minute poll; test 2 observed `map[metric:248]` (freerun) throughout the 10-second sampling window. This is a deterministic regression, not a flake. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Pattern | Suites affected | |---|---| | `GetProfileLogID` / `GetClockIDForeign` log stream timeout | bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc (test 1) | | API server Unauthorized cascade | oc (tests 2–11) | | GNSS → ClockClass 7 holdover state-machine bug | tgm | The log-stream failures are 100% cross-suite (5 of 6 suites, identical error text, different pod names) — confirming a cluster-wide cause rather than a suite-specific issue. The oc Unauthorized failures are oc-specific but are a secondary consequence of the same cluster degradation event. The tgm failures are suite-specific and code-driven. No suite is entirely clean. dualnicbcha is the only suite where the log-stream failure count is below its total suite size (2 of 2 failed; no "passing" baseline in that suite either). --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Re-run the job immediately and monitor the PTP grandmaster.** The log-stream and API-auth failures bear all hallmarks of a transient lab infrastructure event (grandmaster unreachable, credential rotation, API server instability on `telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` around 2026-06-24 07:00–08:30 UTC). The `linuxptp-daemon` does emit this log line (`printNodeProfile` in `pkg/daemon/daemon.go:1369` writes the rendered `Ptp4lConf` with `#profile:` prefix and `message_tag`), but the HTTP connection carrying the pod log stream is dropped before the data reaches the test. The `/var/run/ptp4l.*.config` fallback also failed, meaning the `release-4.19` daemon never wrote or logged the `#profile: <name>` header that the `main`-branch test framework requires. The `release-4.19` nightly image (cut `2026-06-25`) may have diverged from `main`'s `daemon.go:printNodeProfile()` or `config.go:RenderPtp4lConf()` behavior. - **2/22 failures — TGM GNSS simulation code regression (commit `7c915d9`, `main` branch):** - `tgm` suite (2): Commit `7c915d9` ("Replace ublox coldboot with config…") swapped out `ubxtool -p COLDBOOT` for `CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS,50,1`. The u-blox module in time-only mode continues generating 1PPS even without a GNSS fix, so the Intel E810 DPLL never loses its reference and never enters HOLDOVER. Result: ClockClass 248 (FREERUN) instead of ClockClass 7 (HOLDOVER) — both tests fail (`Timed out waiting for clock class 7`; `expected metric [7.000000] but got 248`). --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Root Cause | Suites Affected | Suite-Specific? | |---|---|---| | API server VIP instability | `bc`, `dualnicbcha` | No — same VIP `10.8.34.117:6443`, same run | | `#profile:` log/file missing (release-4.19 daemon) | `dualfollower`, `dualnicbc` | No — identical error string, different pods/nodes | | GNSS simulation regression (`7c915d9`) | `tgm` only | Yes — WPC T-GM hardware path only | - `dualfollower` and `dualnicbc` share an identical failure mode (stream alive, regex never matches) pointing to a `main`-test vs. `release-4.19`-daemon compatibility break. - `dualnicbcha`'s `context deadline exceeded` differs from `dualfollower`/`dualnicbc`'s `timedout waiting for matches`: the stream connection itself died (API instability), not just an absent log line — placing `dualnicbcha` in the infra group. - `bc` is the worst-hit suite but all 11 failures are infrastructure-cascade, not PTP logic. - `tgm` is fully independent of the other suites; its failures would reproduce on a healthy cluster. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Revert `7c915d9` on `main` (fixes 2 tgm failures, unblocks holdover testing):** Restore `ubxtool -p COLDBOOT` (or use the `ubxtool -p RESET` / `ubxtool -z CFG-TP-TIMEPULSE0_ENA,0` approach) to actually cut 1PPS output to the DPLL. `CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS` does not interrupt the hardware clock reference and cannot reliably trigger DPLL HOLDOVER on E810-based T-GM hardware. 2. **Investigate and remediate the API server VIP `10.8.34.117:6443` (fixes 13 failures if cluster is healthy on retry):** Examine the cluster event log for `2026-06-30 ~07:31 UTC` — specifically keepalived/haproxy VIP failover, control-plane node restarts, or etcd leader elections. The entire `bc` suite and `dualnicbcha` suite will pass on a stable cluster. If the cluster is transient CI infra, re-run the job; if it is a persistent lab cluster, file a hardware/infra ticket. 3. **Audit `release-4.19` linuxptp-daemon for `#profile:` emission (fixes 7 failures):** Diff `pkg/daemon/daemon.go` and `pkg/daemon/config.go` between `main` and `release-4.19` around `printNodeProfile()` (line ~819 in `release-4.19`) and `RenderPtp4lConf()` (config.go:437). Additionally, restore `set -e` after the credential-hiding `set +e` block so that image build failures are not silently swallowed. - **Affected tests:** `e2e-telco5g-ptp-telco5g-ptp-tests` (the sole test step; no individual test names extracted because deployment never completed) --- **Relation Between Groups:** Single root cause — the missing CI builder image cascades: build fails → image not pushed → operator pod cannot start → DaemonSet not created → timeout. **Per-Version Notes:** - 4.17: confirmed — `registry.ci.openshift.org/ocp/builder:rhel-9-golang-1.22-openshift-4.17` returns `manifest unknown` - 4.18: same root cause expected — the equivalent tag `rhel-9-golang-1.22-openshift-4.18` is likely also absent; no direct evidence in this run but the failure pattern is identical **Affected Images:** `registry.ci.openshift.org/ocp/builder:rhel-9-golang-1.22-openshift-4.17` (missing); `image-registry.openshift-image-registry.svc:5000/openshift-ptp/ptp-operator:4.17` (never built/pushed) **Overall Issue Class:** `infra_other` **Overall Severity:** HIGH — the entire ptp-operator deployment is blocked on every nightly run until the builder image is restored or the Dockerfile is fixed.

A pre-flight failure would surface infra problems immediately and avoid burning the full ~16-test suite on an unrecoverable testbed state. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `infra_flake` (dominant, ~13/16) + `test_regression` (secondary, ~3/16 — missing backport) **Overall Severity:** HIGH — every parallel suite is blocked by the GNSS infra issue, and the dualnicbcha test regression will recur on every subsequent nightly until the backport lands. --- # Per-Suite Details ## dualfollower --- ### Suite: dualfollower (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single dualfollower failure is the "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" parallel test timing out because the OS clock never reached LOCKED state — a symptom of T-GM (grandmaster) GNSS instability that knocked out all parallel clock-sync tests across every PTP mode simultaneously. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: Delayed phc2sys startup not accounted for in release-4.22 test code** (affects: `[dualnicbcha-serial] PTP ClockSync DualNICBCHA phc2sys switches to secondary ptp4l when primary interface fails`, `[dualnicbcha-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** Serial test (`junit-failures.txt`): ``` could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-5qc8p container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` Parallel test (`junit-failures.txt`): ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` Build log confirming the deployed image is from `release-4.22`: ``` + export PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.22 + PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.22 ``` ``` git clone --single-branch --branch release-4.22 https://github.com/openshift/ptp-operator.git ``` `release-4.22` test code (`git show FETCH_HEAD:test/conformance/serial/ptp.go`): directly calls `GetPodLogsRegex` for phc2sys log immediately, **with no prior wait for ptp4l to reach SLAVE state**: ```go // Get phc2sys logs to identify which interface it's using const phc2sysLogPattern = `phc2sys(?m).*?:.* selecting (\w+) as out-of-domain source clock` logMatches, err := pods.GetPodLogsRegex(fullConfig.DiscoveredClockUnderTestPod.Namespace, ...) ``` `main` branch fix (`bb43e35`) commit message: ``` phc2sys is now intentionally delayed in linuxptp-daemon until the clock source (ptp4l or ts2phc) has synchronized. The DualNICBCHA test was timing out during the 1-minute log search window because phc2sys hadn't started yet. Prerequisite: k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon#215 ``` Delayed startup confirmed in `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/daemon/daemon.go:923-926`: ```go if p.skipInitialStartup != "" { glog.Infof("Delaying %s startup: %s", p.name, p.skipInitialStartup) continue } ``` And cleared only after ptp4l reports sub-second offset (`daemon.go:2094-2131`, `HandleDelayedPhc2sysStartup`). Parallel test fail site (`test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:393`): ```go // The os clock never reach LOCKED status and the test has timed out Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out") ``` Verified `bb43e35` and `402a5b0` are absent from `release-4.22` (30-deep `FETCH_HEAD` log: neither SHA nor their commit messages appear; `release-4.22` HEAD is `48030af`). - **Root Cause:** The OCP 4.22.0-0.nightly-2026-07-22-152558 linuxptp-daemon image includes the intentional delayed-phc2sys-startup feature (introduced by `k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon#215`): phc2sys is held in `skipInitialStartup` state and only launched by `HandleDelayedPhc2sysStartup` once ptp4l reports an offset below 1 second (`daemon.go:2102`). The `release-4.22` test code at `test/conformance/serial/ptp.go` jumps directly to streaming phc2sys logs with a 1-minute timeout, so the context expires before phc2sys has emitted the `selecting … as out-of-domain source clock` line. For the parallel test, phc2sys not running means `CLOCK_REALTIME` never achieves the LOCKED state that `parallel/ptp.go:393` waits for. The fix (`bb43e35`, "test: update CI tests for delayed phc2sys startup") explicitly inserts an `Eventually` gate waiting for the primary BC slave interface to reach SLAVE state before searching phc2sys logs, and is merged to `main` but **not** cherry-picked to `release-4.22`. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** - Linuxptp-daemon behavioral change: `k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon#215` (delayed phc2sys startup — synced into OCP 4.22 nightly) - Missing test fix (main only): https://github.com/openshift/ptp-operator/commit/bb43e35bc01d23cfbb2aa9bee59b4cae193c2021 — NOT cherry-picked to `release-4.22` - Related assertion fix (main only): https://github.com/openshift/ptp-operator/commit/402a5b07852f717809aad6e475323bc1907f0b2d — NOT cherry-picked to `release-4.22` - **Is it a flake?** No — the `release-4.22` test code has no mechanism to wait for phc2sys startup; every run against this image will deterministically time out until the fix is backported. - **Suggested Fix:** Cherry-pick `bb43e35` (and `402a5b0` for the related assertion fix) from `main` into `release-4.22` of `openshift/ptp-operator`. Alternatively, consider backporting `k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon#215`'s context to `release-4.22` documentation so the relationship between daemon behavior and test expectations is explicit. **Suite Issue Class:** test_regression **Suite Severity:** HIGH

The test framework (`ptp-operator-conformance-test`) reads the pod log stream of `linuxptp-daemon-container` and matches regex patterns to extract a "foreign clock ID" and "profile line" — log output produced only when `ptp4l` achieves a master-slave PTP relationship and prints selection messages. Because `ptp4l` never successfully locked to a grandmaster in this run, those log lines never appeared, causing the 204–208 s per-test timeout to fire. The failure is not a code defect in the test regex itself but a genuine failure of PTP synchronization on the worker nodes; this is confirmed by the event-based parallel test below. -
Full analysis
--- ## Cross-Suite Summary — `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-4.20-e2e-telco5g-ptp` **TL;DR:** All 26 failures caused by PTP grandmaster clock never locking on the telco5g lab cluster. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **~22/26 failures — PTP grandmaster never achieved lock; serial log-stream tests timed out** Across every suite (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, and unanalyzed tbc/tgm), the test framework streamed logs from `linuxptp-daemon-container` and waited for a `ptp4l` foreign-master clock-ID line that never appeared. Two variants of the same underlying failure: - `err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded` — stream itself dropped (pod restarting or API server degrading) - `err=timedout waiting for matches` — stream was open but `ptp4l` produced no sync output Affected pods across nodes `cnfdf30`/`cnfdf31`: `linuxptp-daemon-47ghr`, `linuxptp-daemon-f5mqm`, `linuxptp-daemon-45k98`, `linuxptp-daemon-mbltq`, `linuxptp-daemon-kxc27`, `linuxptp-daemon-m8fcv`, `linuxptp-daemon-chgbs`. - **~4/26 failures — OS Clock never reached LOCKED state; parallel event-based tests timed out** Every parallel `[*-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` test in all suites failed with: ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` This is the direct, unambiguous confirmation of the same root cause: the entire clock hierarchy (grandmaster → BC → slave) failed to converge. --- ** Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Pattern | bc | dualfollower | dualnicbc | dualnicbcha | tbc/tgm (inferred) | |---|---|---|---|---|---| | Serial log-stream regex timeout | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | | "OS Clock was never LOCKED" | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | | API server unreachable post-run | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | No suite-specific failures were identified. This did not independently cause test failures but prevented any cleanup or recovery and points to a broader infrastructure degradation event. --- ** --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 bc suite tests failed because ptp4l on the `linuxptp-daemon-container` never achieved PTP clock synchronization (LOCKED state), causing the test harness to time out waiting for expected log-stream regex matches across every BC node configuration. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream read timeout** (affects: "PTP ClockSync Slave can sync to master [Serial]", "PTP ClockSync Downstream slave can sync to BC master [Serial]", "PTP ClockSync Can provide a profile with higher priority [Serial]") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-47ghr container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-f5mqm container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-47ghr container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` Cross-suite confirmation (dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc — all suites): identical `could not find regex in log stream` errors on every linuxptp-daemon pod. - **Root Cause:** The test framework (`ptp-operator-conformance-test`) reads the pod log stream of `linuxptp-daemon-container` and matches regex patterns to extract a "foreign clock ID" and "profile line" — log output produced only when `ptp4l` achieves a master-slave PTP relationship and prints selection messages. Because `ptp4l` never successfully locked to a grandmaster in this run, those log lines never appeared, causing the 204–208 s per-test timeout to fire. The failure is not a code defect in the test regex itself but a genuine failure of PTP synchronization on the worker nodes; this is confirmed by the event-based parallel test below. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no `release-4.20` source changes were detectable from the cloned repos; the systemic cross-suite pattern (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc, tgm all fail identically) makes a single code regression unlikely. - **Is it a flake?** yes — all evidence points to a transient lab/hardware condition rather than a reproducible code defect. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the periodic job; if failures recur across consecutive nightly runs, inspect the telco5g lab's GPS receiver and PTP-capable NIC state on `cnfdf30`/`cnfdf31`, and collect `journalctl` / `ptp4l` logs from inside `linuxptp-daemon-container` to determine whether ptp4l even started negotiating. - **Group 2: OS Clock never LOCKED** (affects: "[bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync") - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` Identical message appears in dualfollower-parallel, dualnicbc-parallel, dualnicbcha-parallel, and tbc-parallel — every parallel event-based test across every suite. - **Root Cause:** The event-based test polls the cloud-event-proxy sidecar for a `PtpClockStateChange` event indicating the OS clock reached `LOCKED`. Because ptp4l never synchronized (same underlying cause as Group 1), no `LOCKED` event was ever published, and the test timed out. This is the direct, unambiguous confirmation that PTP hardware sync failed cluster-wide during this job. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown - **Is it a flake?** yes - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1; additionally verify that `PtpEventsIsConsumerReady` is `true` before the sync tests run (the build log shows `PtpEventsIsConsumerReady= false` in at least one discovered config at `05:50:38`), and confirm the cloud-event-proxy sidecar container was healthy. **Supporting infrastructure context:** - Overall test pod hit the 3h45m prow wall-clock timeout (`Process did not finish before 3h45m0s timeout` at `2026-07-23T05:53:17Z`, killed with `exit status 127`). - Post-test gather step failed with `dial tcp 10.8.34.117:6443: i/o timeout` — the cluster API server became unreachable, indicating cluster-level instability by end of run. - `PtpEventsIsConsumerReady= false` logged at `05:50:38` confirms the event subsystem was not ready, which is consistent with a lab environment that never stabilized. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_timeout **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM (not CRITICAL — this is a known-flaky lab environment; no code regression detected; all basic operator health checks passed) ## dualfollower --- ### Suite: dualfollower (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 dualfollower tests failed because `linuxptp-daemon-45k98` never produced the expected ptp4l sync log lines, a systemic symptom shared across every suite in this job run, pointing to a hardware PTP infrastructure failure on the test cluster. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: ptp4l not producing sync log lines — log stream regex never matches** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Dual follower can sync when one follower port goes down", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-45k98 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-45k98 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` The identical error pattern appears for **every suite** in this job (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc), each against a different daemon pod: ``` linuxptp-daemon-45k98 (dualfollower) linuxptp-daemon-47ghr (bc) linuxptp-daemon-mbltq (dualnicbc) linuxptp-daemon-fmc96 (tbc) ``` The Prow job also hit its global timeout: `"Process did not finish before 3h45m0s timeout"`. Post-test cleanup failed completely: `"error: creating temp namespace: Post "https://10.8.34.117:6443/api/v1/namespaces": dial tcp 10.8.34.117:6443: i/o timeout"` and `"error getting cluster version: Unauthorized"`. - **Root Cause:** The test framework (ptp-operator conformance test suite, `ptphelper.go`) opens a Kubernetes log stream against `linuxptp-daemon-container` and waits for a regex matching a `ptp4l` "foreign master" clock-ID line (e.g., `ptp4l[...]: ... new foreign master <clock_id>`). Two distinct failure modes appear: `context deadline exceeded` means the log API stream itself timed out (pod likely restarting), while `timedout waiting for matches` means the stream was open but ptp4l never emitted the expected pattern. Because this failure is cross-suite and cross-node, ptp4l was not achieving clock synchronization on any of the test nodes — the most likely cause is a failure of the shared hardware PTP grandmaster or network path on the `telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` cluster, exacerbated by the cluster API server at `10.8.34.117` becoming unreachable during the run. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code change is implicated; the deployed images built successfully from `release-4.20` and the cluster was fully provisioned (`Step e2e-telco5g-ptp-telco5g-ptp-cluster-setup succeeded after 2h3m41s`). - **Is it a flake?** yes — this is consistent with a transient hardware/infrastructure outage on the dedicated telco test cluster, not a reproducible code regression. The identical cross-suite failure pattern has no plausible single-PR explanation. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the nightly job; if this recurs across multiple consecutive runs, investigate the health of the telco5g hardware grandmaster clock and network connectivity on the `rdu2` cluster. Check whether the `linuxptp-daemon` pods were OOMKilled or CrashLooping during the run. - **Group 2: OS clock never reached LOCKED state** (affects: "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync") - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` The same message appears in bc-parallel, dualnicbc-parallel, dualnicbcha-parallel, tbc-parallel, and dualfollower-parallel — every parallel event-based suite in this job. - **Root Cause:** The `PTP Slave Clock Sync` parallel test polls the OS clock state (via cloud-event-proxy events or direct phc2sys metric) and waits for it to reach `LOCKED`. Because ptp4l never synced to a grandmaster (same root cause as Group 1), phc2sys could not lock the system clock, causing every instance of this test across all suites to time out. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — same infrastructure root cause as Group 1. - **Is it a flake?** yes - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1; the OS-clock-LOCKED test is a downstream symptom of ptp4l sync failure. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_other **Suite Severity:** HIGH ## dualnicbc --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 failures share a single root cause — PTP clock synchronization never achieved lock, causing every test that streams `linuxptp-daemon-container` logs to time out waiting for a pattern that never appeared. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream never produces expected sync pattern** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-mbltq container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-kxc27 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` (junit-failures.txt lines 61–86; prompt.txt test error text) Additionally, the same "could not find regex in log stream … linuxptp-daemon-container" pattern is present verbatim across **all** other suites run in this job (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbcha, tbc — junit-failures.txt lines 1–141), confirming this is systemic and not isolated to dualnicbc. - **Root Cause:** The test helper (`GetForeignClockID` / `WaitForProfileFromLog` in the test framework) streams pod logs from `linuxptp-daemon-container` and regex-matches for a PTP synchronization line (foreign master clock ID or profile activation). `ptptesthelper.go:106` spawns `pmc -u -b 0 -f /var/run/ptp4l.N.config` in interactive mode via `goexpect` and sends `GET PARENT_DATA_SET\n` (see `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/pmc/pmc.go:17,49,147`). The `bad command: GET` / `bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET` output is pmc's own parser rejecting each token as an unrecognized command. This is consistent with a linuxptp version in the `release-5.0` image where the pmc interactive-mode command table does not include `GET` / `SET` prefixes, or with `management_enabled=0` on ptp4l (added in linuxptp ≥4.2 as the default) causing ptp4l to respond with NOT\_SUPPORTED which pmc renders as "bad command". The `Dockerfile.ocp` (`linuxptp-daemon/addons/redhat/Dockerfile.ocp:12`) installs `linuxptp` from RHEL9 repos with no version pin, so an unintended package update to ≥4.2 is a plausible trigger. -

--- **Job:** `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-5.0-e2e-telco5g-ptp` **Version:** 5.0 | **Total failures:** 32 | **Suites:** dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tgm --- **TL;DR:** All 32 failures trace to a single unlocked T-GM (ts2phc FREERUN) and a broken pmc command interface cascading across every suite. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **~19/32 failures — T-GM ts2phc stuck in FREERUN (clockClass 248) while dpll=LOCKED (tgm: 10, dualnicbc: ~5, dualnicbcha: ~4):** The GM node `cnfdf32` never emits clockClass 6 because `ts2phc_parser.go` accumulates "source ts not valid" hits and forces `ts2phcState = PTP_FREERUN`, overriding a confirmed dpll LOCK. This starves every downstream suite — BC slave ports on the dualnic nodes have no locked upstream grandmaster to sync to, so they remain MASTER/FREERUN themselves, cascading into all WPC GM state, holdover, recovery, and event-timeout failures across all three suites. - **~13/32 failures — pmc rejects `GET PARENT_DATA_SET` as two "bad command" tokens (dualnicbc: 5, dualnicbcha: 4, cross-suite overlap ~4):** Tests calling `ptptesthelper.go:106` via pmc interactive mode receive `bad command: GET` / `bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET` for every query, blocking all clock-identity discovery. The two-token error pattern is diagnostic of either (a) the unpinned `linuxptp` RPM in `Dockerfile.ocp` updating to ≥4.2 where `management_enabled=0` is the new default, or (b) a code path in `pmc.go` passing `"GET PARENT_DATA_SET"` as a single unsplit argument to `exec.Command`. This blocks "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", and "higher priority" tests in both dualnic suites. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Root Cause | dualnicbc | dualnicbcha | tgm | |---|---|---|---| | T-GM FREERUN (ts2phc=0, dpll=1) | Yes (BC slave has no upstream GM) | Yes (BC slave has no upstream GM) | Yes (primary — all WPC tests) | | pmc `bad command: GET / PARENT_DATA_SET` | Yes | Yes | No | - **Shared across all suites:** The absent/unlocked grandmaster. The tgm suite owns the root of this failure; dualnic suites are casualties of it. - **Shared across dualnicbc + dualnicbcha only:** The pmc command-format breakage. Identical error strings, identical affected test names — this is one systemic defect, not two independent bugs. - **Suite-specific:** No failures appear to be genuinely isolated to a single suite; all are manifestations of the two root causes above. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Fix the T-GM ts2phc FREERUN (highest leverage — unblocks ~19 failures across all suites):** On node `cnfdf32`, determine whether a recent `linuxptp` update changed the ts2phc log format so that `regularTS2PhcRegex` in `ts2phc_parser.go:14–23` no longer matches, causing silent `NoSourceTSCount` accumulation. If the regex is the cause, update it to match the new format. Also verify whether commit `fd6ff1e` (upstream-sync, merged 2026-06-30 on `main`) was cherry-picked to `release-5.0` and introduced a regression in the ts2phc state machine. 2. **Fix the pmc `GET PARENT_DATA_SET` command breakage (unblocks ~13 failures in dualnic suites):** Pin the `linuxptp` RPM to a known-working version in `linuxptp-daemon/addons/redhat/Dockerfile.ocp` (currently `yum install linuxptp` with no version). As a parallel fix, verify that `pmc.go` and `ptptesthelper.go:106` pass `GET` and `PARENT_DATA_SET` as separate arguments to `exec.Command` rather than as a single space-delimited string. Additionally, explicitly set `management_enabled 1` in the ptp4l config template in `pkg/daemon/config.go` to prevent linuxptp ≥4.2 from silently disabling management queries. 3. **Investigate whether `fd6ff1e` needs a backport or revert on `release-5.0`:** Run `python scripts/check_backport.py /tmp/ci-investigate fd6ff1e release-5.0` (once available) to confirm whether the 2026-06-30 upstream-sync commit is present in the 5.0 image. If it introduced the ts2phc parser regression or the pmc argument bug, revert or cherry-pick the targeted fix to `release-5.0` rather than carrying the full sync. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` *(Deterministic, reproducible across suites in a single run; not a flake, not an infra timeout — the metrics and log evidence point to a code/package change breaking both the GM state machine and the pmc interface.)* **Overall Severity:** CRITICAL *(The T-GM never locks, meaning the entire PTP sync hierarchy is non-functional; 32/32 failures, all three suites affected, no tests pass in the sync-dependent path.)* --- # Per-Suite Details ## dualnicbc --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (10 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** Every test in the dualnicbc suite fails because the Boundary Clock (BC) slave port `ens2f0np0` never achieves SLAVE state (stuck at clockClass 248 / FREERUN), which cascades into pmc query failures, stale metrics, event timeouts, and outage-recovery timeouts; the T-GM's own `ts2phc` is also in FREERUN, indicating the root failure is the absence of a locked upstream grandmaster. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: pmc returns `bad command: GET` / `bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET`** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` parentPortIdentity.clockIdentity not found in pmc output for /var/run/ptp4l.1.config: bad command: GET bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET grandmasterIdentity not found in pmc output for /var/run/ptp4l.1.config: bad command: GET bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET ``` Identical errors appear for `ptp4l.0.config` in the "higher priority" test, and identically in the dualnicbcha suite on the same node. - **Root Cause:** `ptptesthelper.go:106` spawns `pmc -u -b 0 -f /var/run/ptp4l.N.config` in interactive mode via `goexpect` and sends `GET PARENT_DATA_SET\n` (see `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/pmc/pmc.go:17,49,147`). After GNSS disruption the WPC T-GM DPLL transitions directly to `FREERUN`/`INVALID` instead of `HOLDOVER`, so `updateGMState()` never sets `clockClass = 7`. Could be a `release-4.22` DPLL driver regression (candidate: PR #617 / `fd6ff1e` upstream-sync-2026-06-30) or a lab hardware/firmware issue on the T-GM node. - **2/9 failures — Transient infra flakes** — one per suite (`bc`, `dualnicbcha`): - `bc`: Prometheus cadvisor query returns no valid data after 3 retries for `master3`. - `dualnicbcha`: Kubernetes log-stream returns `context deadline exceeded` before phc2sys HA failover regex is matched. Full analysis
--- **TL;DR:** TBC T-BC discovery is broken (6/9 failures); holdover flakiness and a phc2sys HA timeout account for the rest. **Root Cause Groups:** - **6/9 failures — T-BC discovery broken (`GetInterfaces` reads only `Profile[0]`):** All 6 `tbc` suite tests fail because `api/v1/ptpconfig_webhook.go::GetInterfaces` unconditionally indexes `config.Spec.Profile[0]`, leaving `masterIfCount: 0` and `DiscoveredMasterInterfaces=[]` for the two-profile `test-wpc-tbc` PtpConfig. Introduced by ptp-operator PR #205 (merged 2026-06-23) exposing a pre-existing bug in the webhook. - **2/9 failures — WPC T-GM holdover not reached/sustained (flaky hardware on cnfdf32):** Both `tgm-serial` tests fail because the WPC T-GM on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` either never enters clock class 7 within 3 minutes, or flashes through class 7 so briefly the test cannot observe it before reverting to class 248 (FREERUN). Likely a near-zero `LocalHoldoverTimeout` or unreliable GNSS-disable simulation on this node's firmware. - **1/9 failures — phc2sys HA failover timeout (dualnicbcha):** `phc2sys` does not emit the secondary-interface selection log line within 60 s after `ens2f1` is taken down; `GetPodLogsRegexSince` hits `context deadline exceeded`. Timing-sensitive; possibly a regression from `linuxptp-daemon` upstream sync commit `fd6ff1e` (2026-06-30), but unconfirmed. **Cross-Suite Patterns:** - **Suite-specific:** The `tbc` failure is entirely self-contained — a code bug in the webhook that no other suite triggers. - **Shared pattern (tbc Group 2 + dualnicbcha):** Both `tbc` secondary failures and the `dualnicbcha` failure share the same surface symptom — `context deadline exceeded` from `GetPodLogsRegexSince`/log-stream polling — but for different underlying reasons (broken topology vs. slow daemon response). Not the same root cause. - **tgm is hardware-environment-specific:** The holdover failures are isolated to one physical node (`cnfdf32`) and do not overlap with the software-side failures in the other suites. **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Fix `GetInterfaces` in `api/v1/ptpconfig_webhook.go`** — iterate over all `config.Spec.Profile[:]` entries (not just `[0]`), aggregating master/slave interfaces by `ptpRole`. This is a one-line loop change that immediately unblocks all 6 TBC test failures (67% of the job). 2. **Investigate `cnfdf32` T-GM hardware configuration** — check the `LocalHoldoverTimeout` value in the WPC HardwareConfig CRD on that node, and verify that `ubxtool -z CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS,50,1` reliably simulates GNSS loss on the installed firmware version. If the holdover window is hardware-limited, extend test polling budgets or skip on nodes known to have short holdover. 3. **Add a passing-baseline check for the phc2sys HA failover test** — instrument or log the time phc2sys takes to emit the secondary-interface selection after primary link-down, and compare against the 60 s timeout to determine whether the timeout needs extending or the `linuxptp-daemon` upstream sync introduced a regression in HA switching speed. **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` (primary: code bug in webhook exposed by new TBC tests) + `test_flake` (secondary: hardware holdover and timing sensitivity) **Overall Severity:** HIGH — 6 of 9 failures have a known, fixable root cause with a clear code change; This test has historically been susceptible to this pattern on real hardware. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the job to confirm flakiness. Full analysis
--- **TL;DR:** A single test-code timeout regression blocks clock-sync validation in 20/22 tests; 2 remaining failures expose a known DPLL holdover gap. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **20/22 failures — `GetProfileLogID` log-stream snapshot times out (test regression, commit `41f3dd9`):** Suites bc (3), dualfollower (3), dualnicbc (3), dualnicbcha (3), oc (2), tbc (6). Commit `41f3dd9bf0b33f642d931eb005e54eba89b337fa` ("address gemini review", Feb 26 2026) silently reduced the `GetPodLogsRegex` snapshot context from `TimeoutIn3Minutes` to `TimeoutIn1Minute`. By the time any suite runs (the `linuxptp-daemon` has been up for 3–5 hours), the container's accumulated log volume cannot be streamed within 1 minute; `io.ReadAll` returns `context deadline exceeded`. The fallback follow-stream also fails because the `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` marker was written only at daemon startup and does not reappear. All six affected suites produce the same `GetProfileLogID attempt N failed … error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded / timedout waiting for matches` chain before exhausting the 202-second test deadline. The tbc suite (6 failures) has a compounding factor: `linuxptp-daemon-t68bh` on `cnfdf32` is also crash-looping (suspected duplicate `DiscoveredFollowerInterfaces=[ens7f0 ens7f0]` config), which kills active streams mid-read and amplifies the timeout. - **2/22 failures — DPLL holdover collapses to FREERUN (class 248) without sustaining class 7 (known tech debt):** Suite tgm (2). In `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/dpll/dpll.go:656`, the `DPLL_HOLDOVER` branch immediately checks `!d.inSpec`; if `inSpec` was cleared during the preceding `DPLL_FREERUN` transition, the daemon fires `holdoverCloseCh` and falls to class 248 without ever holding class 7. The code carries an explicit `// TODO: GNSS holdover currently doesn't offer holdover out of spec` comment. Both TGM holdover tests fail deterministically on `cnfdf32`. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Root Cause | Suites affected | Suite-specific? | |---|---|---| | `GetProfileLogID` 1-min timeout regression | bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc, tbc | No — all 6 suites hit identical error path | | tbc crash-loop on `cnfdf32` (compounding) | tbc only | Yes — specific to T-BC node config | | DPLL holdover tech debt | tgm only | Yes — only holdover tests exercise this path | All suites share the identical `ptptesthelper.go:71` → `ptphelper.go:123` → `pods.go:220` call chain. The `oc` suite provided the most complete trace, isolating `41f3dd9` as the breaking commit. The `dualnicbc` per-suite analysis incorrectly attributed the cause to hardware/GM unavailability; the actual mechanism is the same log-stream timeout seen everywhere else. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Revert or fix commit `41f3dd9` in `GetPodLogsRegex` (highest impact — fixes 20/22 failures):** In `test/pkg/pods/pods.go` (around line 220), restore the snapshot context timeout from `TimeoutIn1Minute` back to `TimeoutIn3Minutes` (as set by the prior commit `9c1bc7f`). Alternatively, redesign `GetProfileLogID` to search only the log tail (e.g., `sinceSeconds` or `tailLines` parameter on the Kubernetes log request) rather than re-reading the full accumulated log, making the lookup O(recent output) regardless of daemon uptime. 2. **Investigate and fix the `linuxptp-daemon` crash-loop on `cnfdf32` (fixes tbc amplification):** The `DiscoveredFollowerInterfaces=[ens7f0 ens7f0]` duplicate entry in the T-BC config suggests a profile-discovery bug where the same interface is registered twice. Confirm whether `test-wpc-tbc` profile configuration is correct for node `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com`, and fix the `tbc-tr`/`tbc-tt` interface mapping to prevent the crash-loop that makes log-stream reads impossible even with a corrected timeout. 3. **Address the DPLL holdover tech debt in `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/dpll/dpll.go:656` (fixes tgm failures):** Implement the deferred holdover-in-spec window so that after GNSS loss the daemon sustains `ClockClass7` for the holdover duration before falling to class 248, mirroring the T-BC holdover behavior called out in the existing `TODO` comment. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` (dominant — 20/22 failures caused by `41f3dd9` test-code change; tgm failures are `test_failure` against known gap; tbc crash-loop is `infra_other`) **Overall Severity:** HIGH — a single test-code commit broke clock-sync verification across every PTP topology for the entire job, producing 20 false-negative failures that mask any real regressions in the 4.21 PTP stack. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (3 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All three ClockSync tests fail identically because `GetProfileLogID` cannot find the `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` log entry in the `linuxptp-daemon` pod's log stream, causing the master-clock-ID lookup to time out before sync can be verified. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: Profile log-ID lookup failure — regex mismatch/log rollover** (affects: all 3 failed tests) - **Evidence:** - Test 1 (`Slave can sync to master`, ptpconfig `test-bc-master1`): ``` [FAILED] Timed out after 202.066s. The daemon (`release-4.21`) logs `Ptp4lConf: #profile: test-bc-master1` (from `daemon.go:1131`, `output.profile_name = *nodeProfile.Name`, using the raw spec profile name). The doubled suffix never appears in the log stream, so both Pass 1 (existing-log snapshot) and Pass 2 (follow stream) of `GetPodLogsRegex` fail. The `/var/run/` fallback added by the same co

**TL;DR:** A mid-run control plane outage cascaded into 12 dualnicbc failures, while a main-branch test regression and a hardware flake independently caused the remaining 5. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **~9/17 failures — Control plane progressive outage (dualnicbc):** The API server became unreachable mid-run via i/o timeout, invalidating auth tokens and causing all subsequent tests to fail with `Unauthorized`. One additional failure (`Grandmaster ptpconfig has no associated node`) is a side-effect of pod eviction during the same instability window. - **3/17 failures — Test regression: qualified profile name mismatch (dualfollower):** `ptp-operator` commit `bdc9209` (merged to `main` only) introduced `QualifyProfileName`, producing `"test-slave1_test-slave1"` as the log regex target. The `release-4.19` daemon still emits `"test-slave1"` (unqualified), so the regex never matches, all three dualfollower tests time out at ~202 s. - **3/17 failures — PTP daemon log-stream drops, environment degraded (dualnicbc Group 1):** `context deadline exceeded` and `timedout waiting for matches` errors on the log-stream connection appeared simultaneously across dualnicbc and dualfollower, pointing to a cluster-wide PTP environment degradation (likely grandmaster signal or daemon availability) coincident with the early-stage control plane instability. - **2/17 failures — T-GM clock class stuck at 248 (tgm):** The WPC T-GM node never emitted `CLOCK_CLASS_CHANGE 7.000000` from ptp4l/ts2phc during GNSS signal-loss injection. The node appears to have never reached GNSS-locked state (class 6) before the test triggered loss, so no holdover transition materialized. Hardware-dependent, likely a transient flake. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Pattern | dualfollower | dualnicbc | tgm | |---|---|---|---| | Log-stream regex timeout | Yes (3, test regression) | Yes (3, infra) | No | | Control plane / API server failure | No | Yes (9) | No | | Clock class stuck at 248 | No | No | Yes (2) | - The log-stream timeout error (`could not find regex in log stream … context deadline exceeded / timedout waiting for matches`) is the single most visible symptom across two suites, but the root causes differ: dualfollower's is a **test code regression** (bad regex pattern from `main` branch), while dualnicbc's Group 1 is an **infra connectivity drop**. - The control plane outage is entirely dualnicbc-specific (12 of its failures). - The T-GM clock class issue is entirely tgm-specific. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Fix the test regression immediately (recovers 3 failures, prevents regression from spreading):** Revert or gate `QualifyProfileName` so it is only applied when the target daemon version supports qualified names. Alternatively, add a version check in `GetProfileName` so that against `release-4.19` images the unqualified profile name is used in the regex. This is the only confirmed code bug; it will block every future `dualfollower` run until fixed. 2. **Retry the full job to distinguish infra flakes from real failures:** The 9 dualnicbc control-plane-outage failures and the 3 log-stream drops are consistent with a one-time cluster instability event on 2026-07-07. A clean retry will confirm whether those 12 failures disappear. If the log-stream drops recur without a control-plane event, investigate PTP grandmaster signal availability on the CI cluster. 3. **Investigate T-GM GNSS hardware state before the holdover tests (recovers 2 failures):** Add a pre-test assertion that the T-GM has achieved clock class 6 (GNSS locked) before injecting signal loss. This prevents the test from running in an already-unlocked state and makes the flake detectable rather than a silent timeout. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `infra_other` (dominant — control plane outage caused ~12 failures) + `test_regression` (3 failures from `bdc9209`) + `test_flake` (2 tgm hardware failures) Primary classification: **`infra_other`** **Overall Severity:** HIGH — 12/17 failures from infra mean the suite is not producing reliable signal; --- **TL;DR:** linuxptp-daemon `main` PR #617 broke ts2phc PHC-lock and ptp4l metrics, cascading into 31 of 32 failures across all four suites. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **~19/32 failures — linuxptp-daemon PR #617: ts2phc stuck in FREERUN despite DPLL locked (GM-cascade)** Node `cnfdf32` (WPC T-GM): DPLL hardware reports LOCKED (`state=1`) but ts2phc reports FREERUN (`state=0`). PHC never synchronizes → phc2sys never starts → ptp4l clock class stays 248 → GM never advertises valid Announce messages → BC node `cnfdf32/ens2f0np0` stays in MASTER instead of SLAVE → all downstream BC and slave sync tests fail. Suites: **tgm** (9 tests), **dualnicbc** (~7 tests), **dualnicbcha** (~9 tests, all cascading from this same GM failure) - **3/32 failures — linuxptp-daemon PR #617: `processParsedMetrics` emits `iface="master"` instead of real interface name** New `log_parsing.go` (introduced in PR #617) resolves `ptpMetrics.Iface` via `GetPhcID2IFace("master")` and `GetAlias("master")`, neither of which maps to the real slave interface (`ens2f0np0`). The metric `openshift_ptp_clock_state{iface="master"}` is emitted instead of `{iface="ens2f0np0"}`, so test assertions waiting for the real interface label time out. Suite: **dualfollower** (all 3 tests) - **~3–5/32 failures — ptp-operator commit `3b93c9f`: `getClockIDViaPMC` passes pmc args as separate slice elements** `[]string{..., "GET", "PARENT_DATA_SET"}` should be `[]string{..., "GET PARENT_DATA_SET"}`. pmc's `pmc_do_command` parses both tokens from one string via `sscanf`; passing them separately produces `bad command: GET` / `bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET`. Triggered as a fallback when the BC is already in FREERUN. Suites: **dualnicbc** (Group 1, 3 tests) and **dualnicbcha** (same pmc error visible in evidence) - **1/32 failures — cloud-event-proxy PR #717: log format regression** One tgm test fails log-pattern matching due to a changed log format introduced by PR #717. Suite: **tgm** (1 test) --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Root Cause | dualfollower | dualnicbc | dualnicbcha | tgm | |---|---|---|---|---| | PR #617: ts2phc FREERUN → GM cascade | — | Yes (majority) | Yes (all 9) | Yes (9/10) | | PR #617: wrong `iface` label in metrics | Yes (all 3) | Contributing | — | — | | commit `3b93c9f`: pmc bad args | — | Yes (Group 1) | Yes (overlapping) | — | | cloud-event-proxy PR #717 log format | — | — | — | Yes (1/10) | The **ts2phc FREERUN** failure is the single cross-cutting root cause shared by three of four suites. The pmc bug is shared between **dualnicbc** and **dualnicbcha** but only activates because the BC is already in FREERUN (secondary). The wrong-iface-label bug is **dualfollower-specific** but shares the same PR #617 origin as the ts2phc bug. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Revert or hotfix linuxptp-daemon PR #617 (`fd6ff1e`)** — both sub-bugs originate here. For the ts2phc regression: audit changes in `pkg/daemon/ts2phc.go` and related PHC-lock state propagation introduced in the upstream sync. `getClockIDViaPMC` in `test/pkg/ptphelper/ptphelper.go:144` invokes pmc via `pods.ExecCommand` with the command split as two separate argv entries: `[]string{"pmc", "-b", "0", "-u", "-f", configFile, "GET", "PARENT_DATA_SET"}`. The linuxptp `pmc` binary treats every non-option argument as an independent command token; it processes `"GET"` as a standalone command (returning "bad command: GET") then `"PARENT_DATA_SET"` as another (returning "bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET"). The fix is to pass the command as a single string: `"GET PARENT_DATA_SET"`. This fallback path is only triggered when log-based clock-ID parsing fails; with the BC not locked (Group 2), logs never contain `"selected best master clock"`, so this pmc fallback is always exercised. -

Based on the four suite analyses, here is the cross-suite summary: --- **TL;DR:** GM node stuck in FREERUN and two recent test-code bugs caused all 38 PTP CI failures across dualnicbc, tbc, all, and tgm suites. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **~21/38 failures — GM/BC in FREERUN (clockClass 248), cascading across suites:** `cnfdf32` ts2phc reports servo state s0/s1 (FREERUN) while DPLL is LOCKED, so ptp4l holds clockClass 248, phc2sys never starts, cloud-event-proxy emits no events, and log-stream reads time out. This accounts for 10 tgm failures and at least 6 dualnicbc Group 2 failures (BC upstream port stuck in MASTER because its GM reference is FREERUN). Likely trigger: the "Continuously terminating ts2phc" tgm test destabilized ts2phc and it never re-acquired s2 servo state before subsequent tests ran. - **~3/38 failures — pmc argv split bug (commit `3b93c9f`, Jun 9 2026):** `getClockIDViaPMC` passes `"GET"` and `"PARENT_DATA_SET"` as two separate `exec` argv entries; `pmc` rejects each as `"bad command"`. Only exercised as a fallback when log-based clock-ID parsing fails — which always happens when the BC is not locked (root cause above). Affects three dualnicbc tests: "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority". - **~2/38 failures — No T-BC topology solution in lab cluster (PR #205, commit `84723c8`, Jun 23 2026):** `PtpConfigTelcoBC()` requires a WPC NIC node with three same-NIC interfaces plus a second WPC NIC for local GM; the lab exposes only one such node (`cnfdf32`), already claimed by tgm. The solver returns zero solutions and hard-fails `SynchronizedBeforeSuite`. Affects the tbc suite (1 failure) and the all suite (1 failure, same code path). - **~1/38 failures — `ts2phc` `ProcessRestartCount` Prometheus counter silently reset on PtpConfig delete:** tgm-specific; one test observes the counter drop to zero after a config delete rather than accumulating across restarts. - **~11/38 failures — unaccounted** in the provided per-suite analyses (likely additional dualnicbc Group 2 spillover or suites not detailed here). --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Root Cause | dualnicbc | tbc | all | tgm | |---|---|---|---|---| | GM/BC FREERUN (clockClass 248) | Yes (Group 2, 6+ failures) | — | — | Yes (Group 1, 10 failures) | | pmc argv split bug | Yes (Group 1, 3 failures) | — | — | — | | T-BC no topology solution | — | Yes (1 failure) | Yes (1 failure) | — | | ProcessRestartCount counter reset | — | — | — | Yes (1 failure) | The GM FREERUN root cause is the single largest cross-suite factor, linking tgm and dualnicbc through a shared hardware node (`cnfdf32`). The `release-5.0` linuxptp-daemon publishes `openshift_ptp_clock_state` using an alias computed by `pkg/alias.GetAliasValue()` (`linuxptp-daemon/pkg/utils/utils.go`, regex `^(.+?)(\d+)(?:np\d+)?(\..+)?$`): for `ens2f0np0` and `ens2f3np3` this strips the `npN` suffix and trailing port digit, producing `ens2fx`. The metric is therefore published as `iface="ens2fx"`. The `main`-branch test helper (`test/pkg/metrics/metrics.go:201–222`, commit `f28aceb1`) only tries the raw name (`ens2f0np0`) and a naive last-character alias (`ens2f0npx`); it never tries `ens2fx`. Because `ens2f0np0` and `ens2f3np3` are ports on the same Intel NIC with a single PHC group, the daemon's `calculateAliases` does not fall back to original names (fallback only fires when two distinct PHC IDs share the same alias), so `ens2fx` is the only label emitted. The metric lookup always returns "not found", and each test exhausts its 600 s `Eventually` retry window.
