--- ## Cross-Suite Summary — `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-5.0-e2e-telco5g-ptp` **TL;DR:** The WPC T-GM never achieved GNSS lock (class 248), wiping 22 tests; 3 more failed from a metric alias bug. --- ### Root Cause Groups - **22/25 failures — T-GM stuck at clock class 248 (FREERUN), GNSS lock never achieved** *(suites: tgm 5, dualnicbc 9, dualnicbcha 8)* The WPC grandmaster (`linuxptp-daemon-vzbr9`) never transitioned to clock class 6 for the entire ~32-minute run. With no locked upstream source, every BC's upstream port (`ens2f0np0`) stayed MASTER (BMCA had nothing better to announce), `pmc GET PARENT_DATA_SET` returned `bad command: GET / bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET` on all retries, and every downstream sync/event/metric assertion failed. The prime suspect is commit **`9ba6326`** ("CNF-24996: Decouple GNSS and DPLL", merged to `release-5.0` ~Jun 22) which explicitly removes GNSS state from DPLL state calculation in `pkg/dpll/dpll.go` and `pkg/event/event.go` — breaking the ts2phc → DPLL → ptp4l clock-class-6 promotion pathway for WPC hardware. - **3/25 failures — Interface alias mismatch: daemon labels vs. test query** *(suite: dualfollower 3)* The `release-5.0` linuxptp-daemon computes an alias via `pkg/alias.GetAliasValue()` (regex strips `npN` suffix + trailing digit), publishing `openshift_ptp_clock_state` as `iface="ens2fx"`. The `main`-branch test helper (`test/pkg/metrics/metrics.go:201–222`, commit **`f28aceb1`**) only tries the raw name (`ens2f0np0`) and a naive last-char alias (`ens2f0npx`) — never `ens2fx`. Every metric lookup returns "not found" and each test exhausts its 600s `Eventually` window. --- ### Cross-Suite Patterns | Suite | GM FREERUN | Alias mismatch | |---|---|---| | tgm | YES (direct — GM is the SUT) | — | | dualnicbc | YES (cascaded from GM) | — | | dualnicbcha | YES (cascaded from GM) | — | | dualfollower | Contributing (no upstream sync) | YES (primary label) | - **tgm / dualnicbc / dualnicbcha** share a single root cause: the upstream GM not locking. The failure signature is identical across all three (`clockClass=248`, `role=MASTER`, `bad command: PARENT_DATA_SET`). - **dualfollower** is partially unique: its primary reported failure mode is the alias lookup bug (`ens2fx` not found), but the concurrent GM lock failure means the slaves had no valid source to sync to anyway — both issues compound. - No suite-specific infra or build anomalies were identified beyond these two root causes. --- ### Actionable Recommendations 1. **Investigate/revert `9ba6326` on `release-5.0`** — Run `check_backport.py 9ba6326 release-5.0` to confirm the commit is present, then assess whether re-coupling GNSS state into DPLL state calculation (or providing an alternative signal path) restores clock-class-6 promotion on WPC hardware. This single fix resolves 22/25 failures. 2. **Fix the alias lookup in `test/pkg/metrics/metrics.go`** — Replace the naive `npx` alias candidate with logic matching the daemon's `GetAliasValue()` regex (strip `npN` suffix + trailing port digit, e.g. `ens2f0np0` → `ens2fx`). This resolves 3/25 failures independently of the GM issue. 3. **Verify GNSS signal on the T-GM lab node** — If `9ba6326` is not present on `release-5.0`, rule out a transient hardware/GPS-antenna issue by checking GNSS lock history for `cnfdf31`/`linuxptp-daemon-vzbr9` across adjacent nightly runs. A single-run outage with clean recovery would indicate lab infra rather than a code regression. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` **Overall Severity:** CRITICAL --- # Per-Suite Details ## dualfollower Now I have enough to write the full analysis. --- ### Suite: dualfollower (3 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 3 dualfollower ClockSync tests time out because the test's metric-lookup function cannot find `openshift_ptp_clock_state` for the slave interfaces — the `release-5.0` daemon publishes the aliased iface label `ens2fx`, but the `main`-branch test code only queries `ens2f0np0` and `ens2f0npx`, never `ens2fx`. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: Alias mismatch between daemon metric labels and test metric query** (affects: all 3 tests — "Slave can sync to master", "Dual follower can sync when one follower port goes down", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` ens2f0np0: clock state metric error: metric: openshift_ptp_clock_state, nodeName: cnfdf31.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com, aIf: ens2f0np0 not found, available openshift_ptp_clock_state metrics: [...] ``` (from `junit-failures.txt`, identical for all 3 tests; The underlying incompatibility (daemon aliasing vs. test aliasing) was latent before this PR; that PR did not cherry-pick to `release-5.0`. The daemon-side alias feature in linuxptp-daemon `release-5.0` (`pkg/alias/manager.go::calculateAliases`, `pkg/utils/utils.go::GetAliasValue`) pre-dates this run and is present in the current `release-5.0` build. - **Is it a flake?** No — deterministic regression; the alias mismatch will reproduce on every run with this hardware and these branches. - **Suggested Fix:** In `test/pkg/metrics/metrics.go::getMetric()`, add `GetAliasValue(aIf)` (imported from `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/utils` or reimplemented with the same regex) as a third candidate in `ifCandidates` so the test uses the same aliasing logic as the daemon. The one-liner fix: `ifCandidates = append(ifCandidates, utils.GetAliasValue(aIf))` after the existing `ens2f0npx` candidate.   • 4.22-e2e-telco5g-ptp-upstream (2026-07-06)
The conformance tests open a Kubernetes log stream to the `linuxptp-daemon-container` and look for ptp4l lines containing the foreign-clock-ID (the grandmaster clock identity that ptp4l emits once it reaches SLAVE state, e.g. `FOREIGN_MASTER_RECORD_SET` or similar). When ptp4l is NOT synchronized — because the upstream PTP grandmaster is unavailable or the sync chain broke — those log lines are never produced and the test times out. The fact that every ClockSync test on every suite failed simultaneously against multiple different pods on multiple nodes points to a cluster-wide PTP environment failure (grandmaster or uplink down), not an isolated daemon crash. Configuration/API tests in the same suite (operator deployment, lease, namespace) all passed, confirming the daemon pods were running; only the PTP-sync-dependent tests failed.
Full analysis
**TL;DR:** T-GM GNSS lock loss (ClockClass 248/Freerun) collapsed the entire PTP sync chain, causing all 14 failures across all 5 suites. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **12/14 failures — T-GM GNSS unlocked (ClockClass 248), cascading to all BC/slave ClockSync tests** (suites: bc ×3, dualfollower ×3, dualnicbc ×3, dualnicbcha ×2, tgm ×2): The WPC T-GM node in `telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` lost GNSS lock and, critically, failed to enter holdover (ClockClass 7) — instead transitioning directly to Freerun (ClockClass 248). This is confirmed directly by the tgm suite ("expected a metric [7.000000] but got map[metric:248]", "Timed out waiting for clock class 7") and is the upstream cause of all ClockSync timeouts in every other suite: without a GNSS-locked grandmaster, ptp4l on BC/slave nodes never reaches SLAVE state and the `GetForeignClockID` log-stream helper never matches its regex, firing the 202-second timeout on every test. The same `could not get any profile line / could not find regex in log stream` error message appears across 6 different daemon pods (`jvf75`, `9ssfb`, `z7mfj`, `lkhnx`, `s8frf`, `cklkh`, `h4bbm`) on multiple nodes. - **1/14 failures — Prometheus not returning valid responses during CPU Utilization test** (suite: bc ×1): The parallel CPU Utilization test in `bc` failed because Prometheus was returning no valid data. This is a secondary symptom of the same cluster-health degradation: a lab with a broken PTP grandmaster and, in some suites, API server credential expiry (`Unauthorized` at `07:53:36Z`) is consistent with a broader cluster instability window on 2026-07-03. - **Contributing factor (not a separate root cause) — Kubernetes API server degradation mid-run**: Some suites saw `error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded` (dualnicbcha ×2) rather than the log-stream `timedout waiting for matches` pattern (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc). The API server also emitted `Unauthorized` errors at job teardown. This is a compounding instability but does not change the root cause — the PTP tests would have failed regardless due to the absent grandmaster signal. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Suite | Failures | Root cause | Suite-specific? | |---|---|---|---| | bc | 3 ClockSync + 1 CPU-util | GNSS loss (ClockSync); Prometheus degraded (CPU-util) | No — shared GNSS root | | dualfollower | 3 ClockSync | GNSS loss | No | | dualnicbc | 3 ClockSync | GNSS loss (most direct confirmation via tgm cross-reference) | No | | dualnicbcha | 2 ClockSync | GNSS loss + API server dropping streams | No | | tgm | 2 holdover/event tests | GNSS loss / DPLL not entering holdover | Primary source — not cascading from another suite | All 5 suites share a single upstream root cause. No failure is suite-specific. The tgm suite is the causal origin (DPLL hardware or GNSS disable behavior); all other suites are downstream victims. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Immediately: Verify and restore GNSS lock on the T-GM node.** SSH to the `cnfdf30/cnfdf31/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` T-GM node, check `linuxptp-daemon` logs for GNSS lock status, and confirm the DPLL reports ClockClass 7 before re-triggering the nightly job. Check GNSS antenna connectivity and signal health (GPS signal availability at the lab site). 2. **Investigate DPLL holdover behavior on GNSS loss.** The tgm evidence shows the WPC NIC's DPLL transitions directly from LOCKED → FREERUN (ClockClass 248) without an intermediate HOLDOVER (ClockClass 7) period. This may be caused by the `ubxtool CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS=50` test-disabling mechanism producing an abrupt GNSS cutoff, or the hardware DPLL holdover timeout being shorter than the test's 3-minute polling window. Verify the holdover duration configuration on the WPC NIC and consider whether the test should wait longer or use a softer GNSS-disable method. 3. **Investigate API server token TTL relative to job duration.** The job runs for ~2h50m and the API server was issuing `Unauthorized` errors at teardown. If the CI kubeconfig token has a TTL shorter than the job duration, all long-running nightly jobs will degrade. Rotate or extend the credential lifetime in the `telco5gran` lab CI configuration. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `infra_other` (hardware GNSS failure in the test lab, not a code regression or transient flake) **Overall Severity:** HIGH — 14/14 failures in a nightly periodic job, all suites impacted, no code changes implicated. The job is completely blocked until GNSS lock is restored, but there is no OCP 4.20 code defect to fix. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 3 ClockSync serial tests timed out unable to read the foreign-clock-ID from `linuxptp-daemon-container` pod logs, and the parallel CPU Utilization test failed because Prometheus was not returning valid responses — both symptoms of a systemic infrastructure failure on the test cluster on 2026-07-03. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream returns no foreign-clock-ID output** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-jvf75 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-9ssfb container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-jvf75 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` All three failures occur against different daemon pods (`jvf75` for test-bc-master1/temp, `9ssfb` for test-slave1), confirming the problem spans multiple nodes. The same `could not find regex in log stream` pattern with identical semantics appears in every other suite that ran on the same cluster (dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha) — 9+ failures total with the same root. - **Root Cause:** The conformance tests open a Kubernetes log stream to the `linuxptp-daemon-container` and look for ptp4l lines containing the foreign-clock-ID (the grandmaster clock identity that ptp4l emits once it reaches SLAVE state, e.g. `FOREIGN_MASTER_RECORD_SET` or similar). When ptp4l is NOT synchronized — because the upstream PTP grandmaster is unavailable or the sync chain broke — those log lines are never produced and the test times out. The fact that every ClockSync test on every suite failed simultaneously against multiple different pods on multiple nodes points to a cluster-wide PTP environment failure (grandmaster or uplink down), not an isolated daemon crash. Configuration/API tests in the same suite (operator deployment, lease, namespace) all passed, confirming the daemon pods were running; only the PTP-sync-dependent tests failed. The only candidate code change in the window is `openshift/linuxptp-daemon` commit `b022e1d` (OCPBUGS-95610, "Send ptp4l event when running without proxy", merged 2026-07-02, one day before the run). Inspection of the pre-OCPBUGS-95610 release-4.20 source (`3c31e2f`) shows that this commit adds `tBCTransitionCheck` only in the `!stdoutToSocket` branch (lines 1059-1082 of daemon.go) — the path taken when the cloud-event-proxy (CEP) sidecar is absent. The CI job deploys CEP (`SIDECAR_IMG=...cloud-event-proxy:4.20`), so `stdoutToSocket=true` and the `!stdoutToSocket` block is bypassed entirely. OCPBUGS-95610 is therefore not in the hot path for this job and is ruled out as the cause of these failures. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code change found on `release-4.20` that would affect the `stdoutToSocket=true` (with-CEP) ptp4l sync path. Full analysis
--- **TL;DR:** Nine clock-sync tests fail due to log-stream unavailability; two tgm holdover tests fail due to a bad GNSS-disable method introduced in commit `7c915d9`. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **9/11 failures — PTP daemon log stream unavailable** (dualfollower ×3, dualnicbcha ×2, dualnicbc ×3 implied): Two overlapping sub-causes produce identical `could not find regex in log stream` errors: - *Sub-cause A (dualfollower, likely dualnicbc):* `GetProfileLogID` searches for a one-time startup log line (`Ptp4lConf: #profile: test-slave1`) that has been rotated out of the container log buffer. With `logReduce: false` and `ptp4l` emitting ~16 summary lines/s plus phc2sys at another ~16/s (~6.4 KB/s), the 10 MiB OCP container log limit is exhausted in ≈26 minutes. The test suite ran ≈38 minutes after cluster setup, so the entry is gone before any test starts. - *Sub-cause B (dualnicbcha, likely contributing to dualnicbc):* PTP sync was never established on the hardware (no Grand Master lock → no `master offset` output), compounded by transient Kubernetes API `Unauthorized` errors that dropped the log-streaming connection mid-run (`context deadline exceeded` variant). - Both variants manifest as: first test gets `context deadline exceeded` (Pass 2 stream times out after 3 minutes), later tests get `timedout waiting for matches`. - **1/11 failure — Prometheus unreachable** (dualnicbcha, CPU-utilization test): The test node could not reach the Prometheus endpoint during the CPU-utilization assertion; this is consistent with the broader environment/auth degradation seen in the same run. - **2/11 failures — WPC T-GM DPLL enters FREERUN (class 248) instead of HOLDOVER (class 7)** (tgm ×2): `disableGNSSViaSignalRequirements()` uses `ubxtool CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS,50` to block the navigation solution, but this leaves the DPLL's physical 1PPS reference intact. The DPLL therefore skips HOLDOVER and drops directly to FREERUN (clock class 248). This replaced a reliable coldboot method (`ubxtool -p COLDBOOT`) in commit `7c915d9` (`Replace ublox coldboot with config for min required signal strength`, Nov 17 2025, `main`). --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Suite | Log-stream failure | Prometheus failure | DPLL FREERUN | |---|---|---|---| | dualfollower | ✓ (3/3, log rotation) | — | — | | dualnicbc | ✓ (3/3, implied) | — | — | | dualnicbcha | ✓ (2/3, stream silent + auth) | ✓ (1/3) | — | | tgm | — | — | ✓ (2/2) | - All three clock-sync suites share the same `could not find regex in log stream` failure signature; only the mechanism differs (log rotation vs. PTP sync never established). - The auth/connectivity degradation (`Unauthorized` errors, dropped streams) is cross-suite infrastructure and likely explains why dualnicbc and dualnicbcha also see stream failures even if the startup line itself was present. - The tgm failures are entirely independent — a test-code regression specific to GNSS-disable logic. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Fix log-rotation race (highest impact — unblocks 6–9 failures):** Stop relying on the daemon's one-time startup log line as a test anchor. Either: (a) persist the profile-ID in a Kubernetes ConfigMap or annotation that the test reads via the API, or (b) restart the relevant linuxptp-daemon pod at the start of each suite to guarantee the startup entry is fresh. As a stopgap, set `logReduce: true` in the test PtpConfig to reduce log volume from ~32 lines/s to near-zero background noise, extending log retention beyond the test window. 2. **Restore reliable GNSS disable for T-GM holdover tests (fixes 2 failures):** Revert `7c915d9` or replace `CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS` with a method that actually forces DPLL into HOLDOVER — e.g., restore the `ubxtool -p COLDBOOT` approach or physically disconnect/reconnect the antenna cable in the hardware lab setup. 3. **Investigate and fix environment/auth instability (reduces flakiness):** The `Unauthorized` / `couldn't get current server API group list` errors from the CI operator indicate the cluster's API server credentials expired or the network degraded mid-run. Renew service-account tokens with a longer TTL, or add a pre-test credential-refresh step, to prevent the log-streaming API from being cut off during active tests. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `infra_other` (majority) + `test_regression` (tgm) The 9 log-stream failures are driven by a combination of a fragile test design (relying on ephemeral startup logs) and environment degradation; the 2 tgm failures are a clear test-code regression. **Overall Severity:** HIGH Nine of eleven failures are likely to recur on every run that starts more than ~26 minutes after cluster bring-up, making the suite effectively non-functional in its current state. --- # Per-Suite Details ## dualfollower --- ### Suite: dualfollower (3 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 3 clock-sync tests time out because the linuxptp-daemon's startup configuration log entry (`Ptp4lConf: #profile: test-slave1`) has been rotated out of the Kubernetes container log buffer before the tests attempt to read it, making the critical `GetProfileLogID` regex search permanently fail. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: Daemon startup log unavailable due to container log rotation** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Dual follower can sync when one follower port goes down", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-5c2rz container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-5c2rz container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` From `junit-failures.txt` and from all 3 dualfollower tests (identical pod `linuxptp-daemon-5c2rz`).
